    30→/// Trait for command execution.
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    37→    subcommand: &str,
    38→    args: &[OsString],
    39→    project_root: &Path,
    40→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    41→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    42→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    43→    cmd.args(args);
    44→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    45→    let status = cmd
    46→        .status()
    47→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    48→    if status.success() {
    49→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    50→    } else {
    51→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    52→    }
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package])
    56→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    57→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    58→    get_crate_name(&cwd).ok()
    59→}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     2→use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::Spec;
     7→
     8→/// Load a spec from a TOML file
     9→pub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec> {
    10→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
    11→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
    12→    parse_spec(&content)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Parse a spec from TOML string
    16→pub fn parse_spec(toml_str: &str) -> Result<Spec> {
    17→    toml::from_str(toml_str).context("failed to parse spec TOML")
    18→}
    19→
    20→/// Serialize a spec to TOML string (canonical form for hashing)
    21→pub fn serialize_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    22→    toml::to_string(spec).context("failed to serialize spec")
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Compute hash of a resolved spec (first 8 hex chars)
    26→pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    27→    let toml_str = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    28→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
    29→    hasher.update(toml_str.as_bytes());
    30→    let result = hasher.finalize();
    31→    Ok(hex::encode(&result[..4]))
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Extract spec name from path by stripping the .ts.toml (or .toml) suffix
    35→pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {
    36→    let filename = path
    37→        .file_name()
    38→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    39→        .unwrap_or_default();
    40→
    41→    filename
    42→        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    43→        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    44→        .unwrap_or(&filename)
    45→        .to_string()
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Expand template placeholders in a spec's target_dir field.
    49→/// Returns None if target_dir is absent or empty.
    50→pub fn expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    51→    let raw = match &spec.cargo.target_dir {
    52→        Some(td) if !td.is_empty() => td,
    53→        _ => return Ok(None),
    54→    };
    55→
    56→    let mut expanded = raw.clone();
    57→
    58→    if expanded.contains("<name>") {
    59→        expanded = expanded.replace("<name>", spec_name);
    60→    }
    61→
    62→    if expanded.contains("<hash>") {
    63→        let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
    64→        expanded = expanded.replace("<hash>", &hash);
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    if expanded.is_empty() {
    68→        Ok(None)
    69→    } else {
    70→        Ok(Some(expanded))
    71→    }
    72→}
    73→
    74→/// Save a spec to a TOML file, creating parent directories if needed
    75→pub fn save_spec(spec: &Spec, path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    76→    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
    77→        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
    78→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", parent.display()))?;
    79→    }
    80→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    81→    std::fs::write(path, &content)
    82→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Save a spec snapshot with sequence number and hash: `{name}-{seq:03}-{hash}.toml`
    86→/// Returns the path of the created file.
    87→pub fn save_spec_snapshot(spec: &Spec, name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {
    88→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)
    89→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", dir.display()))?;
    90→
    91→    // Find highest existing sequence number for this name
    92→    let prefix = format!("{}-", name);
    93→    let next_seq = std::fs::read_dir(dir)
    94→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read directory: {}", dir.display()))?
    95→        .filter_map(|entry| entry.ok())
    96→        .filter_map(|entry| {
    97→            let filename = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    98→            if filename.starts_with(&prefix) && filename.ends_with(TSPEC_SUFFIX) {
    99→                // Extract sequence number: {name}-{seq:03}-{hash}{TSPEC_SUFFIX}
   100→                let rest = filename.strip_prefix(&prefix)?;
   101→                let seq_str = rest.split('-').next()?;
   102→                seq_str.parse::<u32>().ok()
   103→            } else {
   104→                None
   105→            }
   106→        })
   107→        .max()
   108→        .map(|n| n + 1)
   109→        .unwrap_or(1);
   110→
   111→    let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
   112→    let filename = format!("{}-{:03}-{}{}", name, next_seq, hash, TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   113→    let path = dir.join(&filename);
   114→
   115→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
   116→    std::fs::write(&path, &content)
   117→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
   118→
   119→    Ok(path)
   120→}
   121→
   122→#[cfg(test)]
   123→mod tests {
   124→    use super::*;
   125→    use crate::test_constants::SUFFIX;
   126→    use crate::types::*;
   127→
   128→    #[test]
   129→    fn parse_empty_spec() {
   130→        let spec = parse_spec("").unwrap();
   131→        assert_eq!(spec.cargo, CargoConfig::default());
   132→        assert_eq!(spec.rustc, RustcConfig::default());
   133→        assert_eq!(spec.linker, LinkerConfig::default());
   134→    }
   135→
   136→    #[test]
   137→    fn hash_is_stable() {
   138→        let spec = Spec::default();
   139→        let hash1 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
   140→        let hash2 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
   141→        assert_eq!(hash1, hash2);
   142→        assert_eq!(hash1.len(), 8);
   143→    }
   144→
   145→    #[test]
   146→    fn different_specs_different_hash() {
   147→        let empty = Spec::default();
   148→        let with_release = Spec {
   149→            cargo: CargoConfig {
   150→                profile: Some(Profile::Release),
   151→                ..Default::default()
   152→            },
   153→            ..Default::default()
   154→        };
   155→        assert_ne!(
   156→            hash_spec(&empty).unwrap(),
   157→            hash_spec(&with_release).unwrap()
   158→        );
   159→    }
   160→
   161→    #[test]
   162→    fn save_and_load_roundtrip() {
   163→        let spec = Spec {
   164→            panic: None,
   165→            strip: None,
   166→            cargo: CargoConfig {
   167→                profile: Some(Profile::Release),
   168→                ..Default::default()
   169→            },
   170→            rustc: RustcConfig {
   171→                lto: Some(true),
   172→                ..Default::default()
   173→            },
   174→            linker: LinkerConfig {
   175→                args: vec!["-static".to_string()],
   176→                ..Default::default()
   177→            },
   178→        };
   179→        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
   180→        let path = dir.path().join("test.toml");
   181→
   182→        save_spec(&spec, &path).unwrap();
   183→        let loaded = load_spec(&path).unwrap();
   184→
   185→        assert_eq!(spec.cargo, loaded.cargo);
   186→        assert_eq!(spec.rustc, loaded.rustc);
   187→        assert_eq!(spec.linker, loaded.linker);
   188→    }
   189→
   190→    #[test]
   191→    fn save_spec_creates_parent_dirs() {
   192→        let spec = Spec::default();
   193→        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
   194→        let path = dir.path().join("nested").join("deep").join("test.toml");
   195→
   196→        save_spec(&spec, &path).unwrap();
   197→        assert!(path.exists());
   198→    }
   199→
   200→    #[test]
   201→    fn snapshot_creates_sequenced_files() {
   202→        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
   203→        let spec1 = Spec::default();
   204→        let spec2 = Spec {
   205→            cargo: CargoConfig {
   206→                profile: Some(Profile::Release),
   207→                ..Default::default()
   208→            },
   209→            ..Default::default()
   210→        };
   211→
   212→        let path1 = save_spec_snapshot(&spec1, "test", dir.path()).unwrap();
   213→        let path2 = save_spec_snapshot(&spec2, "test", dir.path()).unwrap();
   214→        let path3 = save_spec_snapshot(&spec1, "test", dir.path()).unwrap();
   215→
   216→        let name1 = path1.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
   217→        let name2 = path2.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
   218→        let name3 = path3.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
   219→
   220→        assert!(name1.starts_with("test-001-"), "got: {}", name1);
   221→        assert!(name2.starts_with("test-002-"), "got: {}", name2);
   222→        assert!(name3.starts_with("test-003-"), "got: {}", name3);
   223→        assert!(name1.ends_with(SUFFIX), "got: {}", name1);
   224→
   225→        // Same content = same hash suffix (strip prefix and SUFFIX)
   226→        let hash1 = name1
   227→            .strip_prefix("test-001-")
   228→            .unwrap()
   229→            .strip_suffix(SUFFIX)
   230→            .unwrap();
   231→        let hash3 = name3
   232→            .strip_prefix("test-003-")
   233→            .unwrap()
   234→            .strip_suffix(SUFFIX)
   235→            .unwrap();
   236→        assert_eq!(hash1, hash3);
   237→    }

I'm reacquainted with the codebase. Here's a summary:

**tspec** (v0.9.5) — a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust/Cargo.

### Structure
- **CLI** (`cli.rs`) — Clap-based with 11 subcommands: `build`, `run`, `test`, `clean`, `clippy`, `fmt`, `compare`, `ts`, `version`, `install`
- **Commands** (`cmd/`) — Each implements the `Execute` trait. Simple ones (clean, clippy, fmt) delegate via `execute_cargo_subcommand()`. Complex ones (build, run, test, compare, install, version) have custom logic.
- **Translation Specs** (`types.rs`, `tspec.rs`) — TOML-based `*.ts.toml` files with three config sections:
  - `[cargo]` — profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags, target_dir
  - `[rustc]` — opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
  - `[linker]` — args, version_script
  - Plus top-level `panic` and `strip` high-level options
- **Spec management** (`ts_cmd/`) — Subcommands for `tspec ts`: new, hash, list, show, set, backup, restore
- **Build orchestration** (`cargo_build.rs`) — Applies spec settings to cargo invocations
- **Workspace support** (`workspace.rs`, `all.rs`) — Discovers workspace members; batch operations with `--workspace` flag

### Key details
- Rust 2024 edition, conventional commits
- Specs are content-hashed (SHA-256, 8 hex chars) for snapshot naming and target dir isolation
- `target_dir` supports `<name>` and `<hash>` template placeholders
- Currently has some untracked spec files in the project root (t1, tspec variants)

What would you like to work on?

     1→# tspec Design
     2→
     3→## 20260202 - Augment vs Replace Cargo
     4→
     5→**Question:** Should tspec augment or replace cargo?
     6→
     7→**Decision:** Augment cargo (Option 1)
     8→
     9→### Context
    10→
    11→Currently `xt` is designed to *augment* cargo:
    12→- It wraps `cargo build/test/run` with extra configuration (tspec files)
    13→- It generates temporary `build.rs` files for scoped linker flags
    14→- It requires an existing workspace structure
    15→
    16→Running `xt build` outside a workspace fails with "could not find workspace root".
    17→
    18→### Options Considered
    19→
    20→1. **Augment cargo (chosen)** - Stay as a build orchestrator that sits on top of cargo. Users still use cargo directly for simple builds, use tspec when they need spec-driven configuration.
    21→
    22→2. **Replace cargo** - Become a standalone build tool that doesn't require cargo's workspace structure. Much larger scope - would need to handle dependency resolution, compilation, etc.
    23→
    24→3. **Hybrid** - Work standalone for simple cases (single crate) but leverage workspace features when available.
    25→
    26→### Rationale
    27→
    28→Option 1 (augment) is the practical choice. Replacing cargo is a massive undertaking. The "workspace required" limitation can be relaxed by treating a single `Cargo.toml` (Plain Old Package / POP) as a trivial workspace.
    29→
    30→### First Task
    31→
    32→Make tspec work with both workspaces and POPs (Plain Old Packages - single crate projects without a workspace).(**done**)
    33→
    34→## 20260209 - Crate, Package, Cargo.toml
    35→
    36→**Conclusion, tspec provides a mechanism to describe how crates are compiled and linked.**
    37→
    38→### Rust Compilation unit
    39→
    40→General question about rust compiliation units[[1]]
    41→
    42→[1]:https://claude.ai/share/c621b72e-bebe-494a-b9f8-639cbaf10f07
    43→
    44→Crate is the compliation unit of rust.
    45→* Binary crate: produces an executable
    46→* Library crate: produces a library
    47→
    48→Q: a binary crate uses multiple library crated can each library crate have a different tspec?
    49→* yes
    50→* but the final link step needs compatible link settings
    51→  * panic setting must be compatible
    52→  * abi affecting options must be compatible
    53→  * if not the linker should/will produce errors
    54→
    55→Q: does rustc or cargo enforce the defintion of a create?
    56→* rustc
    57→* cargo is just an orchestrator
    58→
    59→Q: So I cannot give rustc a file to compile?
    60→* No, rustc is always given a single file as the root of the crate.
    61→  see formal defintion of crate in the rust reference below [[2]]
    62→
    63→  
    64→Q: If foo.rs is only `fn f() -> i8 { 1 }` it isa crate and can have a tspec.ts.tomal
    65→associated with it to control is compilation?
    66→* yes, but as defined it would be private, is should be `pub fn f() -> i8 { 1 }`
    67→
    68→Q: sym tables (me free thinking wild possibilities here)
    69→* We may want capabilities to control symbol visibility/availability in tspec.
    70→  Symbols are tricky and if we did this it probably would use glob/regex patters
    71→  as matching individual sysmbols would be fragile?
    72→  In anycase we wouldn't want to disallow either case.
    73→
    74→
    75→### Package, Crates and Modules
    76→
    77→[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html):
    78→Rust has a number of features that allow you to manage your
    79→code’s organization, including which details are exposed,
    80→which details are private, and what names are in each scope
    81→in your programs. These features, sometimes collectively
    82→referred to as the module system, include:
    83→
    84→* Packages: A Cargo feature that lets you build, test, and share crates
    85→* Crates: A tree of modules that produces a library or executable
    86→* Modules and use: Let you control the organization, scope, and privacy of paths
    87→* Paths: A way of naming an item, such as a struct, function, or module
    88→
    89→#### Package
    90→
    91→[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-01-packages-and-crates.html)
    92→A package is a bundle of one or more crates that provides a set of functionality. A package contains a Cargo.toml file that describes how to build those crates.
    93→
    94→#### Crate
    95→A rust crate, as stated by [[2]] says; "The Rust compiler is always invoked with a single source file as input, and always produces a single output crate." Thus, a crate is defined by the .rs file passed to the compiler and all of it's dependencies.
    96→
    97→[2]:https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/crates-and-source-files.html
    98→
    99→Crates are imported using `extern crate` [3],
   100→"An extern crate declaration specifies a dependency on an external crate."
   101→
   102→In [The Rust Reference](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference) the term "package" is only found in extern-creates[3] and specifically [here](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html?search=#r-items.extern-crate.name-restrictions)
   103→
   104→So from a language PoV there are crates but no packages. A package is a Cargo concept, not a Rust language concept.
   105→[3](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html)
   106→
   107→#### Modules [ref](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html), [book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html)
   108→
   109→* [intro](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.intro)
   110→  A module is zero or more items.
   111→* [def](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.def)
   112→  A module item is a module, surrounded in braces, named, and prefixed
   113→  with the keyword mod. A module item introduces a new, named module
   114→  into the tree of modules making up a crate.
   115→
   116→
   117→
   118→

> AGENT

Good notes. Your key takeaways are solid:

1. **tspec operates at the crate level** — since rustc always compiles one crate at a time, a tspec describes how a crate is compiled and linked.

2. **Package is a Cargo concept, not a Rust language concept** — the Rust Reference barely mentions "package" at all; it's crates all the way down.

3. **Per-crate tspecs are valid** — a binary crate depending on multiple library crates can each have different tspecs, as long as the final link step has compatible settings (panic strategy, ABI options).

4. **Symbol visibility is an interesting future direction** — you already have `version_script` in `LinkerConfig` with global/local symbol lists; glob/regex patterns would be a natural extension.

One thing worth noting: your current codebase uses "package" terminology in several places (e.g. `--package`/`-p` flags, "Build package(s)" in help text) because that's what Cargo exposes. That's fine — tspec augments Cargo, so it speaks Cargo's language at the CLI level. But conceptually your conclusion is right: what tspec really controls is **crate compilation and linking**.

A few minor typos in the new section if you care: "compliation" → "compilation", "crated" → "crates", "tspec.ts.tomal" → "tspec.ts.toml", "sysmbols" → "symbols", "is should" → "it should". Want me to fix those?

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace crates
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→

    15→/// Result of a batch operation on a single crate
    16→pub struct OpResult {
    17→    pub name: String,
    18→    pub success: bool,
    19→    pub message: String,
    20→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    21→}
    22→
    23→/// Build all workspace crates (excluding build tools)
    24→pub fn build_all(
    25→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    26→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    27→    release: bool,
    28→    strip: bool,
    29→    fail_fast: bool,
    30→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
    31→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    32→
    33→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
    34→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
    35→
    36→        let result = match build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
    37→            Ok(build_result) => {
    38→                if strip
    39→                    && member.has_binary
    40→                    && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
    41→                {
    42→                    eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
    43→                }
    44→                let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
    45→                OpResult {
    46→                    name: member.name.clone(),
    47→                    success: true,
    48→                    message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
    49→                    size,
    50→                }
    51→            }
    52→            Err(e) => OpResult {
    53→                name: member.name.clone(),
    54→                success: false,
    55→                message: e.to_string(),
    56→                size: None,
    57→            },
    58→        };
    59→
    60→        let failed = !result.success;
    61→        results.push(result);
    62→
    63→        if failed && fail_fast {
    64→            break;
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    results
    69→}
    70→
    71→/// Run all app crates sequentially
    72→pub fn run_all(
    73→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    74→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    75→    release: bool,
    76→    strip: bool,
    77→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
    78→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    79→
    80→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
    81→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
    82→
    83→        let result = match build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
    84→            Ok(build_result) => {
    85→                if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
    86→                    eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
    87→                }
    88→                match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
    89→                    Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
    90→                        name: member.name.clone(),
    91→                        success: true, // We don't treat non-zero exit as failure
    92→                        message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
    93→                        size: None,
    94→                    },
    95→                    Err(e) => OpResult {
    96→                        name: member.name.clone(),
    97→                        success: false,
    98→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
    99→                        size: None,
   100→                    },
   101→                }
   102→            }
   103→            Err(e) => OpResult {
   104→                name: member.name.clone(),
   105→                success: false,
   106→                message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   107→                size: None,
   108→            },
   109→        };
   110→
   111→        results.push(result);
   112→    }
   113→
   114→    results
   115→}
   116→
   117→/// Test all workspace crates
   118→pub fn test_all(
   119→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   120→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   121→    release: bool,
   122→    fail_fast: bool,
   123→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   124→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   125→
   126→    // Test regular crates (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   127→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   128→        if member.kind == CrateKind::Test {
   129→            continue; // Handle test crates separately
   130→        }
   131→
   132→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   133→
   134→        let result = match test_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
   135→            Ok(()) => OpResult {
   136→                name: member.name.clone(),
   137→                success: true,
   138→                message: "ok".to_string(),
   139→                size: None,
   140→            },
   141→            Err(e) => OpResult {
   142→                name: member.name.clone(),
   143→                success: false,
   144→                message: e.to_string(),
   145→                size: None,
   146→            },
   147→        };
   148→
   149→        let failed = !result.success;
   150→        results.push(result);
   151→
   152→        if failed && fail_fast {
   153→            return results;
   154→        }
   155→    }
   156→
   157→    // Handle test crates (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   158→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   159→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   160→
   161→        // Build the test crate (builds all binaries)
   162→        let build_result = match build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
   163→            Ok(r) => r,
   164→            Err(e) => {
   165→                results.push(OpResult {
   166→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   167→                    success: false,
   168→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   169→                    size: None,
   170→                });
   171→                if fail_fast {
   172→                    return results;
   173→                }
   174→                continue;
   175→            }
   176→        };
   177→
   178→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   179→        let profile = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
   180→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile);
   181→
   182→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   183→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   184→            .into_iter()
   185→            .flatten()
   186→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   187→            .filter(|e| {
   188→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   189→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   190→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   191→                let is_executable = e
   192→                    .metadata()
   193→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   194→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   195→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   196→            })
   197→            .collect();
   198→
   199→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   200→            results.push(OpResult {
   201→                name: member.name.clone(),
   202→                success: false,
   203→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   204→                size: None,
   205→            });
   206→            continue;
   207→        }
   208→
   209→        for entry in test_binaries {
   210→            let path = entry.path();
   211→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   212→
   213→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   214→
   215→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   216→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   217→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   218→                        println!("ok");
   219→                        OpResult {
   220→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   221→                            success: true,
   222→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   223→                            size: None,
   224→                        }
   225→                    } else {
   226→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   227→                        OpResult {
   228→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   229→                            success: false,
   230→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   231→                            size: None,
   232→                        }
   233→                    }
   234→                }
   235→                Err(e) => {
   236→                    println!("FAILED");
   237→                    OpResult {
   238→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   239→                        success: false,
   240→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   241→                        size: None,
   242→                    }
   243→                }
   244→            };
   245→
   246→            let failed = !result.success;
   247→            results.push(result);
   248→
   249→            if failed && fail_fast {
   250→                return results;
   251→            }
   252→        }
   253→    }
   254→
   255→    results
   256→}
   257→
   258→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   259→pub fn print_test_summary(results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   260→    let max_name_len = results
   261→        .iter()
   262→        .map(|r| r.name.len())
   263→        .max()
   264→        .unwrap_or(5)
   265→        .max(5);
   266→
   267→    println!();
   268→    print_header!("TEST SUMMARY");
   269→    println!("  {:width$}  Status", "Crate", width = max_name_len);
   270→
   271→    let mut passed = 0;
   272→    let mut failed = 0;
   273→
   274→    for result in results {
   275→        let status = if result.success {
   276→            passed += 1;
   277→            "[PASS]"
   278→        } else {
   279→            failed += 1;
   280→            "[FAIL]"
   281→        };
   282→        println!("  {:width$}  {status}", result.name, width = max_name_len);
   283→    }
   284→
   285→    println!();
   286→    println!("  Test: {} passed, {} failed", passed, failed);
   287→    println!();
   288→    println!("  Note: Run `tspec test -p tspec` or `cargo test -p tspec` to test tspec itself");
   289→    print_hline!();
   290→    println!();
   291→
   292→    if failed > 0 {
   293→        ExitCode::from(1)
   294→    } else {
   295→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   296→    }
   297→}
   298→
   299→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   300→pub fn print_summary(results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   301→    let max_name_len = results
   302→        .iter()
   303→        .map(|r| r.name.len())
   304→        .max()
   305→        .unwrap_or(5)
   306→        .max(5);
   307→
   308→    println!();
   309→    print_header!("BUILD SUMMARY");
   310→    println!("  {:width$}  Status    Size", "Crate", width = max_name_len);
   311→
   312→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   313→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   314→
   315→    for result in results {
   316→        let status = if result.success {
   317→            ok_count += 1;
   318→            "[ OK ]"
   319→        } else {
   320→            failed_count += 1;
   321→            "[FAIL]"
   322→        };
   323→        let size_str = result
   324→            .size
   325→            .map(format_size)
   326→            .unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   327→        println!(
   328→            "  {:width$}  {status}  {:>6}",
   329→            result.name,
   330→            size_str,
   331→            width = max_name_len
   332→        );
   333→    }
   334→
   335→    println!();
   336→    println!("  Build: {} ok, {} failed", ok_count, failed_count);
   337→    print_hline!();
   338→    println!();
   339→
   340→    if failed_count > 0 {
   341→        ExitCode::from(1)
   342→    } else {
   343→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   344→    }
   345→}
   346→
   347→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   348→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   349→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   350→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   351→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   352→    } else {
   353→        format!("{}", bytes)
   354→    }
   355→}
   356→
   357→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   358→pub fn print_run_summary(results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   359→    let max_name_len = results
   360→        .iter()
   361→        .map(|r| r.name.len())
   362→        .max()
   363→        .unwrap_or(5)
   364→        .max(5);
   365→
   366→    println!();
   367→    print_header!("RUN SUMMARY");
   368→    println!("  {:width$}  Exit", "Crate", width = max_name_len);
   369→
   370→    let mut error_count = 0;
   371→
   372→    for result in results {
   373→        if result.success {
   374→            // Extract exit code number from message "exit code: X"
   375→            let code = result
   376→                .message
   377→                .strip_prefix("exit code: ")
   378→                .unwrap_or(&result.message);
   379→            println!(
   380→                "  {:width$}  {:>4}",
   381→                result.name,
   382→                code,
   383→                width = max_name_len
   384→            );
   385→        } else {
   386→            error_count += 1;
   387→            println!(
   388→                "  {:width$}  ERROR: {}",
   389→                result.name,
   390→                result.message,
   391→                width = max_name_len
   392→            );
   393→        }
   394→    }
   395→
   396→    println!();
   397→    if error_count > 0 {
   398→        println!("  Run: {} error(s)", error_count);
   399→    }
   400→    print_hline!();
   401→    println!();
   402→
   403→    if error_count > 0 {
   404→        ExitCode::from(1)
   405→    } else {
   406→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   407→    }
   408→}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::io::Write;
     4→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     5→use std::process::Command;
     6→
     7→use crate::find_paths::{
     8→    find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec, get_binary_path, get_binary_path_simple,
     9→    get_crate_name,
    10→};
    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
    12→use crate::types::{OptLevel, PanicStrategy, Profile, Spec};
    13→
    14→/// Result of a successful build
    15→pub struct BuildResult {
    16→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
    17→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
    18→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
    19→}
    20→
    21→/// Build a crate with a spec, returns the binary path on success
    22→pub fn build_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult> {
    23→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    24→    let crate_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, crate_name)?;
    25→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&crate_dir, tspec)?;
    26→
    27→    // Get actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when crate_name is a path)
    28→    let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&crate_dir)?;
    29→
    30→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    31→    let build_rs_path = crate_dir.join("build.rs");
    32→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    33→
    34→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
    35→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    36→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
    37→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    38→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
    39→        (Some(s), td)
    40→    } else {
    41→        (None, None)
    42→    };
    43→
    44→    // Compute paths
    45→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
    46→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
    47→        None => workspace.join("target"),
    48→    };
    49→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
    50→        get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, spec, release, expanded_td.as_deref())
    51→    } else {
    52→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)
    53→    };
    54→
    55→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build
    56→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
    57→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
    58→        println!("Building {} with spec {}", pkg_name, path.display());
    59→
    60→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
    61→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    62→        if has_linker_args && !had_build_rs {
    63→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
    64→        }
    65→
    66→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec)?;
    67→        cmd.arg("build");
    68→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
    69→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    70→
    71→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;
    72→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
    73→    } else {
    74→        println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
    75→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    76→        cmd.arg("build");
    77→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
    78→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    79→        if release {
    80→            cmd.arg("--release");
    81→        }
    82→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
    83→    };
    84→
    85→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
    86→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
    87→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    if !status.success() {
    91→        bail!("cargo build failed");
    92→    }
    93→
    94→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
    95→    Ok(BuildResult {
    96→        binary_path,
    97→        target_base,
    98→    })
    99→}
   100→
   101→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   102→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   103→    let mut lines = vec![
   104→        "// Generated by tspec - do not edit".to_string(),
   105→        "fn main() {".to_string(),
   106→    ];
   107→
   108→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   109→        lines.push(format!(
   110→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   111→            crate_name, arg
   112→        ));
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   116→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   117→    Ok(())
   118→}
   119→
   120→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
   121→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   122→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
   123→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
   124→
   125→    // BuildStd requires nightly
   126→    let has_build_std = !spec.rustc.build_std.is_empty();
   127→
   128→    // Unstable cargo flags require nightly
   129→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   130→
   131→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   132→}
   133→
   134→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   135→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec) -> Result<Command> {
   136→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   137→
   138→    if requires_nightly(spec) {
   139→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   140→    }
   141→
   142→    Ok(cmd)
   143→}
   144→
   145→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command
   146→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   147→    cmd: &mut Command,
   148→    spec: &Spec,
   149→    workspace: &Path,
   150→    release: bool,
   151→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   152→) -> Result<()> {
   153→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   154→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   155→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   156→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   157→    }
   158→
   159→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   160→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   161→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   162→    {
   163→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   164→    }
   165→
   166→    // Handle cargo config
   167→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   168→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   169→        match profile {
   170→            Profile::Release => {
   171→                cmd.arg("--release");
   172→            }
   173→            Profile::Debug => {
   174→                // Debug is default, no flag needed
   175→            }
   176→        }
   177→    }
   178→
   179→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   180→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   181→    }
   182→
   183→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   184→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   185→    }
   186→
   187→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   188→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   189→    }
   190→
   191→    // If no profile in spec but release flag passed, use release
   192→    if !has_profile && release {
   193→        cmd.arg("--release");
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // Collect rustc flags
   197→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   198→
   199→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   200→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   201→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   202→    {
   203→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    // Handle rustc config
   207→    let rustc = &spec.rustc;
   208→
   209→    if let Some(level) = &rustc.opt_level {
   210→        let lvl = match level {
   211→            OptLevel::O0 => "0",
   212→            OptLevel::O1 => "1",
   213→            OptLevel::O2 => "2",
   214→            OptLevel::O3 => "3",
   215→            OptLevel::Os => "s",
   216→            OptLevel::Oz => "z",
   217→        };
   218→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C opt-level={}", lvl));
   219→    }
   220→
   221→    if let Some(strategy) = &rustc.panic {
   222→        let s = match strategy {
   223→            PanicStrategy::Abort => "abort",
   224→            PanicStrategy::Unwind => "unwind",
   225→            PanicStrategy::ImmediateAbort => "immediate-abort",
   226→        };
   227→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", s));
   228→    }
   229→
   230→    if let Some(true) = rustc.lto {
   231→        rustc_flags.push("-C lto=true".to_string());
   232→    }
   233→
   234→    if let Some(n) = rustc.codegen_units {
   235→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C codegen-units={}", n));
   236→    }
   237→
   238→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   239→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   240→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   241→    {
   242→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   243→    }
   244→
   245→    if !rustc.build_std.is_empty() {
   246→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   247→        let crates_str = rustc.build_std.join(",");
   248→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   249→    }
   250→
   251→    for flag in &rustc.flags {
   252→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   253→    }
   254→
   255→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   256→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   257→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   258→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   259→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   260→        };
   261→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   262→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   263→
   264→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   265→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   266→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   267→
   268→        let mut f =
   269→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   270→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   271→
   272→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   273→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   274→            version_script_path.display()
   275→        ));
   276→    }
   277→
   278→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   279→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   280→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   281→    }
   282→
   283→    Ok(())
   284→}
   285→

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::Args;
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::{Execute, current_package_name};
     7→use crate::all::{build_all, print_summary};
     8→use crate::binary::strip_binary;
     9→use crate::cargo_build::build_crate;
    10→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    11→
    12→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    13→#[derive(Args)]
    14→pub struct BuildCmd {
    15→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    16→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    17→    pub package: Option<String>,
    18→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    19→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    20→    pub workspace: bool,
    21→    /// Translation spec to use (defaults to package's tspec file)
    22→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    23→    pub tspec: Option<String>,
    24→    /// Release build
    25→    #[arg(short, long)]
    26→    pub release: bool,
    27→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    28→    #[arg(short, long)]
    29→    pub strip: bool,
    30→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    31→    #[arg(short, long)]
    32→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    33→}
    34→
    35→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    36→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    37→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p PKG > cwd > all
    38→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    39→            None
    40→        } else {
    41→            self.package.clone().or_else(current_package_name)
    42→        };
    43→
    44→        match resolved {
    45→            None => {
    46→                // Build all packages
    47→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    48→                let results = build_all(
    49→                    &workspace,
    50→                    self.tspec.as_deref(),
    51→                    self.release,
    52→                    self.strip,
    53→                    self.fail_fast,
    54→                );
    55→                Ok(print_summary(&results))
    56→            }
    57→            Some(name) => {
    58→                let result = build_crate(&name, self.tspec.as_deref(), self.release)?;
    59→                if self.strip {
    60→                    strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    61→                }
    62→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→    }
    66→}
    67→

     1→//! Workspace discovery and crate classification
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members and classify them
     4→//! by type (app, lib, tool, test, build tool).
     5→
     6→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     7→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→/// Crate classification for behavior differences
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    12→pub enum CrateKind {
    13→    /// apps/* - application binaries, runnable
    14→    App,
    15→    /// libs/* - library crates
    16→    Lib,
    17→    /// tools/* - utility binaries
    18→    Tool,
    19→    /// */tests - test binary crates (special handling)
    20→    Test,
    21→    /// Build tools such as xtask or tspec, excluded by default
    22→    BuildTool,
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Information about a workspace member
    26→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    27→pub struct CrateMember {
    28→    pub name: String,
    29→    pub path: PathBuf,
    30→    pub has_binary: bool,
    31→    pub kind: CrateKind,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    35→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    36→    pub root: PathBuf,
    37→    pub members: Vec<CrateMember>,
    38→}
    39→
    40→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    41→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    42→    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {
    43→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    44→            .no_deps()
    45→            .exec()
    46→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    47→
    48→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    49→
    50→        let packages = metadata.workspace_packages();
    51→        let is_pop = packages.len() == 1;
    52→
    53→        let members: Vec<CrateMember> = packages
    54→            .iter()
    55→            .map(|pkg| {
    56→                let path = pkg
    57→                    .manifest_path
    58→                    .parent()
    59→                    .unwrap()
    60→                    .as_std_path()
    61→                    .to_path_buf();
    62→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    63→                // POPs are always App — classify_crate is for multi-crate workspaces
    64→                let kind = if is_pop {
    65→                    CrateKind::App
    66→                } else {
    67→                    classify_crate(&path, &pkg.name)
    68→                };
    69→
    70→                CrateMember {
    71→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    72→                    path,
    73→                    has_binary,
    74→                    kind,
    75→                }
    76→            })
    77→            .collect();
    78→
    79→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    80→    }
    81→
    82→    /// Get members excluding build tools such as xtask or tspec
    83→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&CrateMember> {
    84→        self.members
    85→            .iter()
    86→            .filter(|m| m.kind != CrateKind::BuildTool)
    87→            .collect()
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    /// Get members that can be run (apps only - have binaries and are runnable)
    91→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&CrateMember> {
    92→        self.members
    93→            .iter()
    94→            .filter(|m| m.has_binary && m.kind == CrateKind::App)
    95→            .collect()
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    /// Get test crates (special handling for rlibc-x2-tests etc.)
    99→    pub fn test_members(&self) -> Vec<&CrateMember> {
   100→        self.members
   101→            .iter()
   102→            .filter(|m| m.kind == CrateKind::Test)
   103→            .collect()
   104→    }
   105→}
   106→
   107→/// Classify a crate based on its path and name
   108→fn classify_crate(path: &Path, name: &str) -> CrateKind {
   109→    let path_str = path.to_string_lossy();
   110→
   111→    // Build tools at workspace root level
   112→    if name == "tspec" || name == "xt" || name == "xtask" {
   113→        return CrateKind::BuildTool;
   114→    }
   115→
   116→    // Test crates
   117→    if path_str.contains("/tests") || name.ends_with("-tests") {
   118→        return CrateKind::Test;
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    // Categorize by directory
   122→    if path_str.contains("apps/") {
   123→        CrateKind::App
   124→    } else if path_str.contains("libs/") {
   125→        CrateKind::Lib
   126→    } else if path_str.contains("tools/") {
   127→        CrateKind::Tool
   128→    } else {
   129→        // Unknown location, treat as build tool (excluded)
   130→        CrateKind::BuildTool
   131→    }
   132→}
   133→
   134→#[cfg(test)]
   135→mod tests {
   136→    use super::*;
   137→
   138→    #[test]
   139→    fn classify_app() {
   140→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/apps/ex-x1");
   141→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "ex-x1"), CrateKind::App);
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    #[test]
   145→    fn classify_lib() {
   146→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/libs/rlibc-x1");
   147→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "rlibc-x1"), CrateKind::Lib);
   148→    }
   149→
   150→    #[test]
   151→    fn classify_tool() {
   152→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/tools/is-libc-used");
   153→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "is-libc-used"), CrateKind::Tool);
   154→    }
   155→
   156→    #[test]
   157→    fn classify_test_by_path() {
   158→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/libs/rlibc-x2/tests");
   159→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "rlibc-x2-tests"), CrateKind::Test);
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    #[test]
   163→    fn classify_test_by_name() {
   164→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/somewhere");
   165→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "foo-tests"), CrateKind::Test);
   166→    }
   167→
   168→    #[test]
   169→    fn classify_build_tool_xt() {
   170→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/xt");
   171→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "xt"), CrateKind::BuildTool);
   172→    }
   173→
   174→    #[test]
   175→    fn classify_build_tool_xtask() {
   176→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/xtask");
   177→        assert_eq!(classify_crate(&path, "xtask"), CrateKind::BuildTool);
   178→    }
   179→
   180→    #[test]
   181→    fn discover_works() {
   182→        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs
   183→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();
   184→        if let Ok(info) = info {
   185→            // Should have some members (at least 1 for POP, more for workspace)
   186→            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());
   187→
   188→            let buildable = info.buildable_members();
   189→            if info.members.len() == 1 {
   190→                // POP: the single crate should be buildable
   191→                assert_eq!(buildable.len(), 1);
   192→            } else {
   193→                // Workspace: xt and xtask should be excluded from buildable
   194→                assert!(
   195→                    buildable
   196→                        .iter()
   197→                        .all(|m| m.name != "xt" && m.name != "xtask")
   198→                );
   199→            }
   200→
   201→            // For workspaces with apps/, should have runnable members
   202→            // For POPs, the single crate is runnable if it has a binary
   203→            let _runnable = info.runnable_members();
   204→            // Just verify it doesn't panic - count depends on project structure
   205→        }
   206→        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI
   207→    }
   208→}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use glob::Pattern;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::{Profile, Spec};
     7→
     8→/// Check if TOML content has an exact section header like `[workspace]` or `[package]`
     9→/// Only matches lines where the trimmed content equals `[section]` exactly
    10→fn has_toml_section_exact(content: &str, section: &str) -> bool {
    11→    let header = format!("[{}]", section);
    12→    content.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == header)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Extract crate name from Cargo.toml
    16→pub fn get_crate_name(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    17→    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    18→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
    20→
    21→    // Simple parsing - look for name = "..." in [package] section
    22→    let mut in_package = false;
    23→    for line in content.lines() {
    24→        let line = line.trim();
    25→        if line == "[package]" {
    26→            in_package = true;
    27→            continue;
    28→        }
    29→        if line.starts_with('[') {
    30→            in_package = false;
    31→            continue;
    32→        }
    33→        if in_package
    34→            && line.starts_with("name")
    35→            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
    36→        {
    37→            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
    38→            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    39→            return Ok(value.to_string());
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    bail!("could not find package name in {}", cargo_toml.display());
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    49→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    50→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    51→
    52→    loop {
    53→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    54→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    55→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    56→            // Workspace takes precedence
    57→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    58→                return Ok(dir);
    59→            }
    60→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    61→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    62→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        if !dir.pop() {
    66→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    67→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    68→                return Ok(root);
    69→            }
    70→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");
    71→        }
    72→    }
    73→}
    74→
    75→/// Check if a project root is a POP (Plain Old Package) vs a workspace
    76→pub fn is_pop(project_root: &Path) -> bool {
    77→    let cargo_toml = project_root.join("Cargo.toml");
    78→    if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml) {
    79→        // POP has [package] but no [workspace]
    80→        has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package")
    81→            && !has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace")
    82→    } else {
    83→        false
    84→    }
    85→}
    86→
    87→/// Resolve package directory from optional name, defaulting to current directory
    88→/// If package is None, uses current directory (must contain Cargo.toml)
    89→/// If package is Some, looks up the package by name
    90→pub fn resolve_package_dir(workspace: &Path, package: Option<&str>) -> Result<PathBuf> {
    91→    match package {
    92→        Some(name) => find_package_dir(workspace, name),
    93→        None => {
    94→            let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    95→            if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
    96→                Ok(cwd)
    97→            } else {
    98→                bail!(
    99→                    "not in a package directory (no Cargo.toml found). Use -p to specify a package."
   100→                )
   101→            }
   102→        }
   103→    }
   104→}
   105→
   106→/// Find a package's directory - tries as path first, then searches standard locations
   107→/// For POPs, checks if name matches the root package
   108→pub fn find_package_dir(project_root: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   109→    // Try as path first (relative or absolute)
   110→    let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   111→    if as_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   112→        return Ok(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    // For POPs, check if name matches the root package
   116→    if is_pop(project_root) {
   117→        if let Ok(pkg_name) = get_crate_name(project_root)
   118→            && pkg_name == name
   119→        {
   120→            return Ok(project_root.to_path_buf());
   121→        }
   122→        bail!(
   123→            "package '{}' not found (this is a single-package project with package '{}')",
   124→            name,
   125→            get_crate_name(project_root).unwrap_or_else(|_| "unknown".to_string())
   126→        );
   127→    }
   128→
   129→    // Workspace: search libs/, apps/, tools/
   130→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps", "tools"] {
   131→        let path = project_root.join(prefix).join(name);
   132→        if path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   133→            return Ok(path);
   134→        }
   135→    }
   136→
   137→    // Special case: nested test crates like libs/rlibc-x2/tests
   138→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps"] {
   139→        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(project_root.join(prefix))
   140→            .into_iter()
   141→            .flatten()
   142→            .flatten()
   143→        {
   144→            let nested = entry.path().join("tests");
   145→            if nested.join("Cargo.toml").exists()
   146→                && let Ok(pkg_name) = get_crate_name(&nested)
   147→                && pkg_name == name
   148→            {
   149→                return Ok(nested);
   150→            }
   151→        }
   152→    }
   153→
   154→    bail!("package '{}' not found", name);
   155→}
   156→
   157→/// Find the tspec for a crate - tries as path first, then relative to crate_dir
   158→/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)
   159→pub fn find_tspec(crate_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {
   160→    match explicit {
   161→        Some(name) => {
   162→            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   163→            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   164→            if as_path.exists() {
   165→                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));
   166→            }
   167→
   168→            // Fallback: relative to crate directory
   169→            let in_crate = crate_dir.join(name);
   170→            if in_crate.exists() {
   171→                return Ok(Some(in_crate));
   172→            }
   173→
   174→            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension
   175→            if !name.contains('.') {
   176→                let with_suffix = crate_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   177→                if with_suffix.exists() {
   178→                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));
   179→                }
   180→            }
   181→
   182→            bail!("tspec not found: {}", name);
   183→        }
   184→        None => {
   185→            let default = crate_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   186→            if default.exists() {
   187→                Ok(Some(default))
   188→            } else {
   189→                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build
   190→            }
   191→        }
   192→    }
   193→}
   194→
   195→/// Find multiple tspecs by glob patterns
   196→/// If no patterns given, defaults to "tspec*{TSPEC_SUFFIX}"
   197→/// Returns sorted list of paths, errors if none found
   198→pub fn find_tspecs(crate_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {
   199→    let default_pattern = format!("tspec*{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   200→    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {
   201→        vec![&default_pattern]
   202→    } else {
   203→        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   204→    };
   205→
   206→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   207→
   208→    for pattern in &patterns {
   209→        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   210→        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   211→        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {
   212→            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   213→            continue;
   214→        }
   215→
   216→        // Try as literal path relative to crate_dir
   217→        let in_crate = crate_dir.join(pattern);
   218→        if in_crate.exists() && in_crate.is_file() {
   219→            results.push(in_crate);
   220→            continue;
   221→        }
   222→
   223→        // Try as glob pattern in crate_dir
   224→        let glob_pattern =
   225→            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!("invalid glob pattern: {}", pattern))?;
   226→
   227→        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(crate_dir)
   228→            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read directory: {}", crate_dir.display()))?
   229→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   230→            .filter(|e| {
   231→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   232→                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)
   233→            })
   234→            .map(|e| e.path())
   235→            .collect();
   236→
   237→        results.extend(entries);
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    // Remove duplicates and sort
   241→    results.sort();
   242→    results.dedup();
   243→
   244→    if results.is_empty() {
   245→        let pattern_list = patterns.join(", ");
   246→        bail!(
   247→            "no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}",
   248→            pattern_list,
   249→            crate_dir.display()
   250→        );
   251→    }
   252→
   253→    Ok(results)
   254→}
   255→
   256→/// Get binary path for a build with a spec
   257→pub fn get_binary_path(
   258→    workspace: &Path,
   259→    crate_name: &str,
   260→    spec: &Spec,
   261→    release: bool,
   262→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   263→) -> PathBuf {
   264→    let is_release = release || spec.cargo.profile.as_ref() == Some(&Profile::Release);
   265→
   266→    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {
   267→        spec.cargo
   268→            .target_json
   269→            .as_ref()
   270→            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))
   271→    });
   272→
   273→    let profile_dir = if is_release { "release" } else { "debug" };
   274→
   275→    let base = match expanded_target_dir {
   276→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   277→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   278→    };
   279→
   280→    match target {
   281→        Some(t) => base.join(t).join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),
   282→        None => base.join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),
   283→    }
   284→}
   285→
   286→/// Get binary path for a simple build (no spec)
   287→pub fn get_binary_path_simple(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, release: bool) -> PathBuf {
   288→    let profile_dir = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
   289→    workspace.join("target").join(profile_dir).join(crate_name)
   290→}

   559→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&default_name), "# default").unwrap();
   560→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&opt_name), "# opt").unwrap();
   561→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("other.toml"), "# other").unwrap();
   562→
   563→        // Empty patterns = default "tspec*{SUFFIX}"
   564→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[]).unwrap();
   565→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
   566→        assert!(
   567→            found
   568→                .iter()
   569→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == default_name.as_str())
   570→        );
   571→        assert!(
   572→            found
   573→                .iter()
   574→                .any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == opt_name.as_str())
   575→        );

   585→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", SUFFIX)), "# default").unwrap();
   586→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&opt_name), "# opt").unwrap();
   587→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(format!("other{}", SUFFIX)), "# other").unwrap();

   598→        fs::create_dir(&crate_dir).unwrap();

   601→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("b.toml"), "# b").unwrap();
   602→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("c.toml"), "# c").unwrap();
   603→
   604→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &["a.toml".to_string(), "c.toml".to_string()]).unwrap();
   605→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
   606→        assert!(found.iter().any(|p| p.file_name().unwrap() == "a.toml"));

   610→    #[test]
   611→    fn find_tspecs_sorted_and_deduped() {
   612→        let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   613→        let crate_dir = tmp.path().join("crate");
   614→        fs::create_dir(&crate_dir).unwrap();
   615→
   616→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("z.toml"), "# z").unwrap();
   617→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("a.toml"), "# a").unwrap();
   618→
   619→        // Request same file twice via different patterns

> AGENT

Yes, you have that correct. Here's the flow:

1. **`build_all`** — calls `WorkspaceInfo::discover()`, which runs `cargo metadata` and gets back **packages** (Cargo's concept). It iterates over those and calls `build_crate()` for each.

2. **Single package** — user passes `-p tspec`, `build_crate("tspec", ...)` is called, which calls `find_package_dir()` then `find_tspec()` looking for a `tspec.ts.toml` in that **package directory**.

3. **`build_crate()`** itself then does `cargo build -p <pkg_name>` — that's a package-level operation.

The granularity is locked to Cargo's package concept throughout:
- Tspec files live in the package directory
- The `-p` flag maps to `cargo build -p`
- `RUSTFLAGS` env var applies to the entire `cargo build` invocation

So if a package contains **multiple crates** (e.g. both `src/main.rs` and `src/lib.rs`, or multiple `[[bin]]` targets), they all get the same tspec. You can't give `src/lib.rs` different compilation flags than `src/main.rs` within the same package.

That said, for the common case this isn't really a limitation — most packages have one primary crate, and when you need different compilation settings you typically split into separate packages (which is what workspaces are for). The place where true per-crate control would matter is controlling flags for **dependency crates** (third-party or workspace libs), and that's something Cargo itself doesn't expose well either (`RUSTFLAGS` is all-or-nothing per invocation).

Is this a tension you want to address, or more of an observation you're documenting?

> AGENT

Right, good observation. You can see the two distinct rustc invocations clearly — same `--crate-name tspec` but different root files and crate types:

1. `src/lib.rs --crate-type lib` → produces `libtspec-b4722f6801f89092.rlib`
2. `src/main.rs --crate-type bin` → consumes that `.rlib` as `--extern tspec=...libtspec-b4722f6801f89092.rlib`

So yes, two crates, and today they both get whatever `RUSTFLAGS` tspec sets.

But here's the practical constraint: **Cargo doesn't give you per-crate control within a package.** When you run `cargo build -p tspec`, Cargo decides the build plan and invokes rustc twice. The only knobs you have are:

- `RUSTFLAGS` — applies to **all** rustc invocations in the build
- `cargo rustc -p tspec -- <flags>` — applies flags only to the **final** crate (the bin), not the lib
- Generated `build.rs` with `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` — linker args scoped to a specific binary

So to truly associate different tspecs with lib.rs vs main.rs within the same package, you'd have to either:
1. **Invoke rustc directly** — bypass Cargo entirely, which is a massive scope change
2. **Split the package** — move lib.rs into its own package so each has its own `cargo build -p` invocation (this is the idiomatic Rust answer)
3. **Use `cargo rustc`** — limited, only customizes the "last" crate

For tspec's own case, do you actually *need* different flags for lib vs bin? The bin is a thin wrapper (45 lines of main.rs) that just calls into the lib. The interesting compilation settings (optimization, LTO, panic) would naturally be the same for both.

I think the real question is: what's the use case where you'd want different specs for crates within the same package, vs just splitting into separate packages?

> AGENT

Quick terminology note: Cargo calls those `[lib]`/`[[bin]]` entries **targets**, not members. "Members" is workspace-level (packages in the workspace). So within a package you have targets, within a workspace you have members.

Both approaches could work, but they have very different trade-offs:

**Approach 1: Multiple targets in one package, per-target tspecs**

You'd switch from `cargo build -p foo` to multiple `cargo rustc` calls:
- `cargo rustc -p foo --lib -- <lib-flags>`
- `cargo rustc -p foo --bin bar -- <bin-flags>`

The problem: `cargo rustc` applies the extra flags only to the *named target*, not its deps. But `RUSTFLAGS` (which tspec currently uses) hits everything. So you'd need to stop using `RUSTFLAGS` and pass everything through `cargo rustc -- <flags>` instead. And you'd need to manage build ordering yourself (lib before bin). You're basically becoming a mini build orchestrator on top of Cargo's orchestrator.

**Approach 2: Separate packages (one crate per package)**

This works *today* with zero changes to tspec. Each package gets its own directory, `Cargo.toml`, and `tspec.ts.toml`. Workspace `members` ties them together. It's idiomatic Rust — this is exactly why workspaces exist.

My take: approach 2 is the right answer. It's what Cargo was designed for, tspec already supports it, and it keeps the model clean: **one package = one primary crate = one tspec**. Approach 1 is fighting Cargo's design for marginal benefit. If someone needs different compilation settings for their lib vs bin, the Rust answer is "make them separate packages."
