     1→# tspec Design
     2→
     3→## 20260202 - Augment vs Replace Cargo
     4→
     5→**Question:** Should tspec augment or replace cargo?
     6→
     7→**Decision:** Augment cargo (Option 1)
     8→
     9→### Context
    10→
    11→Currently `xt` is designed to *augment* cargo:
    12→- It wraps `cargo build/test/run` with extra configuration (tspec files)
    13→- It generates temporary `build.rs` files for scoped linker flags
    14→- It requires an existing workspace structure
    15→
    16→Running `xt build` outside a workspace fails with "could not find workspace root".
    17→
    18→### Options Considered
    19→
    20→1. **Augment cargo (chosen)** - Stay as a build orchestrator that sits on top of cargo. Users still use cargo directly for simple builds, use tspec when they need spec-driven configuration.
    21→
    22→2. **Replace cargo** - Become a standalone build tool that doesn't require cargo's workspace structure. Much larger scope - would need to handle dependency resolution, compilation, etc.
    23→
    24→3. **Hybrid** - Work standalone for simple cases (single crate) but leverage workspace features when available.
    25→
    26→### Rationale
    27→
    28→Option 1 (augment) is the practical choice. Replacing cargo is a massive undertaking. The "workspace required" limitation can be relaxed by treating a single `Cargo.toml` (Plain Old Package / POP) as a trivial workspace.
    29→
    30→### First Task
    31→
    32→Make tspec work with both workspaces and POPs (Plain Old Packages - single crate projects without a workspace).(**done**)
    33→
    34→## 20260209 - Crate, Package, Cargo.toml
    35→
    36→**Conclusion, tspec provides a mechanism to describe how crates are compiled and linked.**
    37→
    38→### Rust Compilation unit
    39→
    40→General question about rust compilation units[[1]]
    41→
    42→[1]:https://claude.ai/share/c621b72e-bebe-494a-b9f8-639cbaf10f07
    43→
    44→Crate is the compilation unit of rust.
    45→* Binary crate: produces an executable
    46→* Library crate: produces a library
    47→
    48→Q: a binary crate uses multiple library crates can each library crate have a different tspec?
    49→* yes
    50→* but the final link step needs compatible link settings
    51→  * panic setting must be compatible
    52→  * abi affecting options must be compatible
    53→  * if not the linker should/will produce errors
    54→
    55→Q: does rustc or cargo enforce the definition of a crate?
    56→* rustc
    57→* cargo is just an orchestrator
    58→
    59→Q: So I cannot give rustc a file to compile?
    60→* No, rustc is always given a single file as the root of the crate.
    61→  see formal definition of crate in the rust reference below [[2]]
    62→
    63→  
    64→Q: If foo.rs is only `fn f() -> i8 { 1 }` it is a crate and can have a tspec.ts.toml
    65→associated with it to control its compilation?
    66→* yes, but as defined it would be private, it should be `pub fn f() -> i8 { 1 }`
    67→
    68→Q: sym tables (me free thinking wild possibilities here)
    69→* We may want capabilities to control symbol visibility/availability in tspec.
    70→  Symbols are tricky and if we did this it probably would use glob/regex patterns
    71→  as matching individual symbols would be fragile?
    72→  In any case we wouldn't want to disallow either case.
    73→
    74→
    75→### Package, Crates and Modules
    76→
    77→[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html):
    78→Rust has a number of features that allow you to manage your
    79→code’s organization, including which details are exposed,
    80→which details are private, and what names are in each scope
    81→in your programs. These features, sometimes collectively
    82→referred to as the module system, include:
    83→
    84→* Packages: A Cargo feature that lets you build, test, and share crates
    85→* Crates: A tree of modules that produces a library or executable
    86→* Modules and use: Let you control the organization, scope, and privacy of paths
    87→* Paths: A way of naming an item, such as a struct, function, or module
    88→
    89→#### Package
    90→
    91→[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-01-packages-and-crates.html)
    92→A package is a bundle of one or more crates that provides a set of functionality. A package contains a Cargo.toml file that describes how to build those crates.
    93→
    94→#### Crate
    95→A rust crate, as stated by [[2]] says; "The Rust compiler is always invoked with a single source file as input, and always produces a single output crate." Thus, a crate is defined by the .rs file passed to the compiler and all of its dependencies.
    96→
    97→[2]:https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/crates-and-source-files.html
    98→
    99→Crates are imported using `extern crate` [3],
   100→"An extern crate declaration specifies a dependency on an external crate."
   101→
   102→In [The Rust Reference](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference) the term "package" is only found in extern-crates[3] and specifically [here](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html?search=#r-items.extern-crate.name-restrictions)
   103→
   104→So from a language PoV there are crates but no packages. A package is a Cargo concept, not a Rust language concept.
   105→[3](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html)
   106→
   107→#### Modules [ref](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html), [book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html)
   108→
   109→* [intro](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.intro)
   110→  A module is zero or more items.
   111→* [def](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.def)
   112→  A module item is a module, surrounded in braces, named, and prefixed
   113→  with the keyword mod. A module item introduces a new, named module
   114→  into the tree of modules making up a crate.
   115→

A module item introduces a new, named module\n  into the tree of modules making up a crate.\n\n### Package vs Crate granularity in tspec\n\n**Question:** tspec currently operates at the package level, but conceptually\nit describes crate compilation. How do we handle packages with multiple crates?\n\n**Decision:** One package = one tspec. If you need per-crate control, split into\nseparate packages.\n\n#### Current behavior\n\ntspec operates at Cargo's package granularity:\n- `build_all` discovers workspace **packages** via `cargo metadata` and iterates,\n  calling `build_crate()` (misnamed — actually builds a package) for each\n- Single package: user passes `-p <name>`, `build_crate()` runs `cargo build -p <name>`\n- `RUSTFLAGS` is set per invocation, applying to all crates within the package\n\nA package can contain multiple crates (targets). For example, tspec itself has two:\n- `src/lib.rs` → library crate (`--crate-type lib`)\n- `src/main.rs` → binary crate (`--crate-type bin`)\n\nBoth get the same `RUSTFLAGS` from a single `cargo build -p tspec` invocation.\n\n#### Terminology\n\nCargo calls `[lib]`/`[[bin]]` entries **targets**, not members.\n\"Members\" is workspace-level (packages in the workspace).\nWithin a package you have targets, within a workspace you have members.\n\n#### Options considered for per-crate tspecs\n\n**1. Per-target tspecs within a package** — Use Cargo.toml `[lib]`/`[[bin]]`\ntarget names to find `<target-name>.ts.toml`, then invoke `cargo rustc`\nper target instead of `cargo build -p`.\n\nProblems:\n- `cargo rustc --lib -- <flags>` applies extra flags only to the named target,\n  not its dependencies, but `RUSTFLAGS` (which tspec currently uses) hits everything\n- Would need to stop using `RUSTFLAGS` and pass everything via `cargo rustc -- <flags>`\n- Would need to manage build ordering (lib before bin) — becoming a mini build\n  orchestrator on top of Cargo's orchestrator\n- Fighting against Cargo's design\n\n**2. Separate packages (chosen)** — If you need different compilation settings\nfor lib vs bin, split them into separate packages. Each gets its own directory,\n`Cargo.toml`, and `tspec.ts.toml`. Workspace `members` ties them together.\n\nThis works today with zero changes to tspec. It's idiomatic Rust — this is\nexactly why workspaces exist.\n\n#### Rationale\n\nApproach 2 keeps the model clean: **one package = one primary crate = one tspec**.\nApproach 1 fights Cargo's design for marginal benefit. For the common case\n(tspec itself: thin main.rs calling into lib.rs), there's no reason to compile\nthe lib and bin differently. When you truly need different settings, the Rust\nanswer is \"make them separate packages.\"\n\n"
}

diff --git a/notes/tspec-design.md b/notes/tspec-design.md
index 5b0a33d..af056fa 100644

+++ b/notes/tspec-design.md
@@ -29,4 +29,147 @@ Option 1 (augment) is the practical choice. Replacing cargo is a massive underta
 
 ### First Task
 
-Make tspec work with both workspaces and POPs (Plain Old Packages - single crate projects without a workspace).
+Make tspec work with both workspaces and POPs (Plain Old Packages - single crate projects without a workspace).(**done**)
+
+## 20260209 - Crate, Package, Cargo.toml
+
+**Conclusion, tspec provides a mechanism to describe how crates are compiled and linked.**
+
+### Rust Compilation unit
+
+General question about rust compilation units[[1]]
+
+[1]:https://claude.ai/share/c621b72e-bebe-494a-b9f8-639cbaf10f07
+
+Crate is the compilation unit of rust.
+* Binary crate: produces an executable
+* Library crate: produces a library
+
+Q: a binary crate uses multiple library crates can each library crate have a different tspec?
+* yes
+* but the final link step needs compatible link settings
+  * panic setting must be compatible
+  * abi affecting options must be compatible
+  * if not the linker should/will produce errors
+
+Q: does rustc or cargo enforce the definition of a crate?
+* rustc
+* cargo is just an orchestrator
+
+Q: So I cannot give rustc a file to compile?
+* No, rustc is always given a single file as the root of the crate.
+  see formal definition of crate in the rust reference below [[2]]
+
+  
+Q: If foo.rs is only `fn f() -> i8 { 1 }` it is a crate and can have a tspec.ts.toml
+associated with it to control its compilation?
+* yes, but as defined it would be private, it should be `pub fn f() -> i8 { 1 }`
+
+Q: sym tables (me free thinking wild possibilities here)
+* We may want capabilities to control symbol visibility/availability in tspec.
+  Symbols are tricky and if we did this it probably would use glob/regex patterns
+  as matching individual symbols would be fragile?
+  In any case we wouldn't want to disallow either case.
+
+
+### Package, Crates and Modules
+
+[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html):
+Rust has a number of features that allow you to manage your
+code’s organization, including which details are exposed,
+which details are private, and what names are in each scope
+in your programs. These features, sometimes collectively
+referred to as the module system, include:
+
+* Packages: A Cargo feature that lets you build, test, and share crates
+* Crates: A tree of modules that produces a library or executable
+* Modules and use: Let you control the organization, scope, and privacy of paths
+* Paths: A way of naming an item, such as a struct, function, or module
+
+#### Package
+
+[book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-01-packages-and-crates.html)
+A package is a bundle of one or more crates that provides a set of functionality. A package contains a Cargo.toml file that describes how to build those crates.
+
+#### Crate
+A rust crate, as stated by [[2]] says; "The Rust compiler is always invoked with a single source file as input, and always produces a single output crate." Thus, a crate is defined by the .rs file passed to the compiler and all of its dependencies.
+
+[2]:https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/crates-and-source-files.html
+
+Crates are imported using `extern crate` [3],
+"An extern crate declaration specifies a dependency on an external crate."
+
+In [The Rust Reference](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference) the term "package" is only found in extern-crates[3] and specifically [here](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html?search=#r-items.extern-crate.name-restrictions)
+
+So from a language PoV there are crates but no packages. A package is a Cargo concept, not a Rust language concept.
+[3](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/items/extern-crates.html)
+
+#### Modules [ref](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html), [book](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/book/ch07-00-managing-growing-projects-with-packages-crates-and-modules.html)
+
+* [intro](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.intro)
+  A module is zero or more items.
+* [def](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/items/modules.html#r-items.mod.def)
+  A module item is a module, surrounded in braces, named, and prefixed
+  with the keyword mod. A module item introduces a new, named module
+  into the tree of modules making up a crate.
+
+#### Cargo terminology
+
+* **Targets** — `[lib]`, `[[bin]]`, `[[example]]`, `[[test]]`, `[[bench]]`
+  sections in Cargo.toml. These are the crates within a package.
+* **Members** — packages listed in `[workspace] members` in the root Cargo.toml.
+
+Within a package you have targets, within a workspace you have members.
+
+### Package vs Crate granularity in tspec
+
+**Question:** tspec currently operates at the package level, but conceptually
+it describes crate compilation. How do we handle packages with multiple crates?
+
+**Decision:** One package = one tspec. If you need per-crate control, split into
+separate packages.
+
+#### Current behavior
+
+tspec operates at Cargo's package granularity:
+- `build_all` discovers workspace packages via `cargo metadata` and iterates,
+  calling `build_crate()` (misnamed — actually builds a package) for each
+- Single package: user passes `-p <name>`, `build_crate()` runs `cargo build -p <name>`
+- `RUSTFLAGS` is set per invocation, applying to all crates within the package
+
+A package can contain multiple crates (targets). For example, tspec itself has two:
+- `src/lib.rs` → library crate (`--crate-type lib`)
+- `src/main.rs` → binary crate (`--crate-type bin`)
+
+Both get the same `RUSTFLAGS` from a single `cargo build -p tspec` invocation.
+
+#### Options considered for per-crate tspecs
+
+**1. Per-target tspecs within a package** — Use Cargo.toml `[lib]`/`[[bin]]`
+target names to find `<target-name>.ts.toml`, then invoke `cargo rustc`
+per target instead of `cargo build -p`.
+
+Problems:
+- `cargo rustc --lib -- <flags>` applies extra flags only to the named target,
+  not its dependencies, but `RUSTFLAGS` (which tspec currently uses) hits everything
+- Would need to stop using `RUSTFLAGS` and pass everything via `cargo rustc -- <flags>`
+- Would need to manage build ordering (lib before bin) — becoming a mini build
+  orchestrator on top of Cargo's orchestrator
+- Fighting against Cargo's design
+
+**2. Separate packages (chosen)** — If you need different compilation settings
+for lib vs bin, split them into separate packages. Each gets its own directory,
+`Cargo.toml`, and `tspec.ts.toml`. Workspace `members` ties them together.
+
+This works today with zero changes to tspec. It's idiomatic Rust — this is
+exactly why workspaces exist.
+
+#### Rationale
+
+Approach 2 keeps the model clean: **one package = one primary crate = one tspec**.
+Approach 1 fights Cargo's design for marginal benefit. For the common case
+(tspec itself: thin main.rs calling into lib.rs), there's no reason to compile
+the lib and bin differently. When you truly need different settings, the Rust
+answer is "make them separate packages."
+
+
```

     1→use std::path::Path;
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→
     5→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
     6→use crate::cargo_build::build_crate;
     7→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
     8→
     9→/// Result of building a spec
    10→struct SpecResult {
    11→    name: String,
    12→    size: u64,
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Compare multiple specs for a crate
    16→pub fn compare_specs(
    17→    crate_name: &str,
    18→    spec_paths: &[impl AsRef<Path> + std::fmt::Debug],
    19→    release: bool,
    20→    strip: bool,
    21→) -> Result<()> {
    22→    println!(
    23→        "Comparing {} builds{}:\n",
    24→        crate_name,
    25→        if strip { " (stripped)" } else { "" }
    26→    );
    27→    //println!("Using specs: {:?}", spec_paths);
    28→
    29→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    30→
    31→    for spec_path in spec_paths {
    32→        let spec_path = spec_path.as_ref();
    33→        let name = spec_path
    34→            .file_name()
    35→            .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    36→            .unwrap_or_else(|| spec_path.display().to_string());
    37→
    38→        let size = build_spec(crate_name, spec_path, release, strip)?;
    39→        results.push(SpecResult { name, size });
    40→        println!();
    41→    }
    42→
    43→    // Sort by size (smallest first)
    44→    results.sort_by_key(|r| r.size);
    45→
    46→    print_comparison(&results);
    47→
    48→    Ok(())
    49→}
    50→
    51→fn build_spec(crate_name: &str, spec_path: &Path, release: bool, strip: bool) -> Result<u64> {
    52→    let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
    53→    println!(
    54→        "  {}:",
    55→        spec_path.file_name().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy()
    56→    );
    57→
    58→    // Build
    59→    let build_result = build_crate(crate_name, Some(&spec_str), release)?;
    60→
    61→    // Optionally strip
    62→    if strip {
    63→        strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    64→    }
    65→
    66→    // Get size
    67→    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)?;
    68→    println!("    size: {} bytes", format_size(size));
    69→
    70→    Ok(size)
    71→}
    72→
    73→fn print_comparison(results: &[SpecResult]) {
    74→    let largest_size = results.iter().map(|r| r.size).max().unwrap_or(0);
    75→    let max_name_len = results.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(4);
    76→
    77→    // Format percent change: show reduction with minus sign, baseline as 0.0%
    78→    let fmt_pct = |size: u64| -> String {
    79→        if largest_size == 0 {
    80→            return "   0.0%".to_string();
    81→        }
    82→        let pct = ((largest_size as f64 - size as f64) / largest_size as f64) * 100.0;
    83→        if pct > 0.0 {
    84→            format!("{:>7.1}%", -pct)
    85→        } else {
    86→            "   0.0%".to_string()
    87→        }
    88→    };
    89→
    90→    println!();
    91→    print_header!("COMPARE SUMMARY");
    92→    println!(
    93→        "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {:>8}",
    94→        "Spec",
    95→        "Size",
    96→        "Change",
    97→        width = max_name_len
    98→    );
    99→    for result in results {
   100→        println!(
   101→            "  {:width$}  {:>10}  {}",
   102→            result.name,
   103→            format_size(result.size),
   104→            fmt_pct(result.size),
   105→            width = max_name_len
   106→        );
   107→    }
   108→    print_hline!();
   109→    println!();
   110→}
   111→
   112→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   113→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   114→        format!("{:.3}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   115→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   116→        format!("{:.3}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   117→    } else {
   118→        format!("{}", bytes)
   119→    }
   120→}
   121→

    10→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
    11→
    12→/// Build package(s) with a translation spec
    13→#[derive(Args)]
    14→pub struct BuildCmd {
    15→    /// Package to build (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    16→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    17→    pub package: Option<String>,
    18→    /// Build all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    19→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    20→    pub workspace: bool,
    21→    /// Translation spec to use (defaults to package's tspec file)
    22→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    23→    pub tspec: Option<String>,
    24→    /// Release build
    25→    #[arg(short, long)]
    26→    pub release: bool,
    27→    /// Strip symbols from binary after build
    28→    #[arg(short, long)]
    29→    pub strip: bool,
    30→    /// Stop on first failure (for all-packages mode)
    31→    #[arg(short, long)]
    32→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    33→}
    34→
    35→impl Execute for BuildCmd {
    36→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    37→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p PKG > cwd > all
    38→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    39→            None
    40→        } else {
    41→            self.package.clone().or_else(current_package_name)
    42→        };
    43→
    44→        match resolved {
    45→            None => {
    46→                // Build all packages
    47→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    48→                let results = build_all(
    49→                    &workspace,
    50→                    self.tspec.as_deref(),
    51→                    self.release,
    52→                    self.strip,
    53→                    self.fail_fast,
    54→                );
    55→                Ok(print_summary(&results))
    56→            }
    57→            Some(name) => {
    58→                let result = build_crate(&name, self.tspec.as_deref(), self.release)?;
    59→                if self.strip {
    60→                    strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    61→                }
    62→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→    }
    66→}
    67→

    14→#[derive(Args)]
    15→pub struct RunCmd {
    16→    /// Package to run (defaults to current directory or all apps)
    17→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    18→    pub package: Option<String>,
    19→    /// Run all workspace apps (even when in a package directory)
    20→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    21→    pub workspace: bool,
    22→    /// Translation spec to use (defaults to package's tspec file)
    23→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    24→    pub tspec: Option<String>,
    25→    /// Release build
    26→    #[arg(short, long)]
    27→    pub release: bool,
    28→    /// Strip symbols from binary before running
    29→    #[arg(short, long)]
    30→    pub strip: bool,
    31→    /// Arguments to pass to the binary (after --)
    32→    #[arg(last = true)]
    33→    pub args: Vec<String>,
    34→}
    35→
    36→impl Execute for RunCmd {
    37→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    38→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p PKG > cwd > all
    39→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    40→            None
    41→        } else {
    42→            self.package.clone().or_else(current_package_name)
    43→        };
    44→
    45→        match resolved {
    46→            None => {
    47→                // Run all apps (args not supported for --workspace)
    48→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    49→                let results = run_all(&workspace, self.tspec.as_deref(), self.release, self.strip);
    50→                Ok(print_run_summary(&results))
    51→            }
    52→            Some(name) => {
    53→                // Build, optionally strip, then run
    54→                let result = build_crate(&name, self.tspec.as_deref(), self.release)?;
    55→                if self.strip {
    56→                    strip_binary(&result.binary_path)?;
    57→                }
    58→                let exit_code = run_binary(&result.binary_path, &self.args)?;
    59→                std::process::exit(exit_code);
    60→            }
    61→        }
    62→    }
    63→}
    64→

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::process::Command;
     4→
     5→use crate::cargo_build::{apply_spec_to_command, generate_build_rs};
     6→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec};
     7→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
     8→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
     9→fn requires_nightly(spec: &crate::types::Spec) -> bool {
    10→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
    11→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
    12→
    13→    let has_build_std = !spec.rustc.build_std.is_empty();
    14→
    15→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
    16→
    17→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
    18→}
    19→
    20→/// Test a crate with a spec
    21→pub fn test_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<()> {
    22→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    23→    let crate_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, crate_name)?;
    24→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&crate_dir, tspec)?;
    25→
    26→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    27→    let build_rs_path = crate_dir.join("build.rs");
    28→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    29→
    30→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo test
    31→    let status = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    32→        let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    33→        let spec_name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    34→        let expanded_td = expand_target_dir(&spec, &spec_name)?;
    35→        println!("Testing {} with spec {}", crate_name, path.display());
    36→
    37→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
    38→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    39→        if has_linker_args && !had_build_rs {
    40→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, crate_name, &spec)?;
    41→        }
    42→
    43→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    44→        if requires_nightly(&spec) {
    45→            cmd.arg("+nightly");
    46→        }
    47→        cmd.arg("test");
    48→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(crate_name);
    49→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    50→
    51→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;
    52→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?
    53→    } else {
    54→        println!("Testing {} (no tspec)", crate_name);
    55→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    56→        cmd.arg("test");
    57→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(crate_name);
    58→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    59→        if release {
    60→            cmd.arg("--release");
    61→        }
    62→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?
    63→    };
    64→
    65→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
    66→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
    67→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
    68→    }
    69→
    70→    if !status.success() {
    71→        bail!("cargo test failed");
    72→    }
    73→
    74→    Ok(())
    75→}
    76→

    14→    /// Package to test (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    15→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    16→    pub package: Option<String>,
    17→    /// Test all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    18→    #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]

    21→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]

    24→    #[arg(short, long)]
    25→    pub release: bool,
    26→    /// Stop on first failure
    27→    #[arg(short, long)]
    28→    pub fail_fast: bool,
    29→}
    30→
    31→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    32→    fn execute(&self, _project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    33→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p PKG > cwd > all
    34→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    35→            None
    36→        } else {
    37→            self.package.clone().or_else(current_package_name)
    38→        };
    39→
    40→        match resolved {
    41→            None => {

    44→                let results = test_all(
    45→                    &workspace,

    47→                    self.release,
    48→                    self.fail_fast,

    50→                Ok(print_test_summary(&results))
    51→            }
    52→            Some(name) => {

    55→            }
    56→        }
    57→    }
    58→}

```
   101→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   102→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   103→    let mut lines = vec![
   104→        "// Generated by tspec - do not edit".to_string(),
   105→        "fn main() {".to_string(),
   106→    ];
   107→
   108→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   109→        lines.push(format!(
   110→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   111→            crate_name, arg
   112→        ));
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   116→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   117→    Ok(())
   118→}
   119→

> AGENT

The `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=<name>` directive takes a **binary target name**, which happens to be the package name in the common case. It's passed `pkg_name` from both callers now. I'll rename the parameter to `bin_name` since that's what it actually represents in the `build.rs` context — but actually, the callers pass the package name and it works because the binary target name typically matches. Renaming to `pkg_name` here would be misleading. Let me leave `generate_build_rs` as-is since `crate_name` is arguably correct there (it's the binary crate's name for the linker directive).

   130→separate packages.
   131→
   132→#### Current behavior
   133→
   134→tspec operates at Cargo's package granularity:
   135→- `build_all` discovers workspace packages via `cargo metadata` and iterates,
   136→  calling `build_crate()` (misnamed — actually builds a package) for each
   137→- Single package: user passes `-p <name>`, `build_crate()` runs `cargo build -p <name>`
   138→- `RUSTFLAGS` is set per invocation, applying to all crates within the package
   139→

id: toolu_01Li9P8BJaWVpp1dSTCYNbWH
```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Usage
    19→
    20→```bash
    21→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    22→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    23→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    24→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    25→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbol with tspec.ts.toml if present
    26→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    27→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    28→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    29→tspec compare -p myapp -r                  # Compare all tspec*.ts.toml by binary size
    30→```
    31→
    32→The `-p` flag specifies a package (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages).
    33→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    34→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically.
    35→
    36→## tspec Files
    37→
    38→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    39→
    40→```toml
    41→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    42→
    43→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    44→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    45→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    46→
    47→[cargo]
    48→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release"
    49→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    50→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (mutually exclusive with target_triple)
    51→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    52→target_dir = "<name>"                      # per-spec target dir; supports <name> and <hash> placeholders
    53→
    54→[rustc]
    55→opt_level = "z"                            # "0", "1", "2", "3", "s", "z"
    56→panic = "abort"                            # "abort", "unwind", "immediate-abort" (prefer top-level panic)
    57→lto = true
    58→codegen_units = 1
    59→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    60→flags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw rustc flags passed through
    61→
    62→[linker]
    63→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
    64→
    65→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
    66→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
    67→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
    68→```
    69→
    70→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically. If both
    71→top-level `panic` and `rustc.panic` are set, both are applied (prefer one or the other).
    72→
    73→### Key Concepts
    74→
    75→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
    76→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
    77→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs
    78→
    79→### How Linker Flags Work
    80→
    81→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
    82→
    83→```rust
    84→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
    85→fn main() {
    86→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
    87→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
    88→}
    89→```
    90→
    91→This is necessary because RUSTFLAGS affects ALL compilations (including dependency
    92→build scripts), causing crashes with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated
    93→build.rs scopes flags to just the binary.
    94→
    95→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
    96→
    97→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing:
    98→
    99→```bash
   100→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   101→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List all tspec files for a package
   102→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents, i.e. *.ts.toml
   103→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   104→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   105→tspec ts hash -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show content hash of tspec-opt.ts.toml
   106→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml
   107→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   108→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec creating opt2.ts.toml
   109→tspec ts set strip=symbols -p myapp      # Set value in tspec.ts.toml
   110→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Create versioned backup (name-NNN-hash.ts.toml)
   111→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup myapp/tspec-opt.ts.toml to myapp/tspec-opt-<next-seqnum>-<hash>.ts.toml
   112→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup t1-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   113→```
   114→
   115→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   116→
   117→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   118→
   119→## Testing
   120→
   121→```bash
   122→cargo test                    # Run all tests
   123→cargo test -- --nocapture     # See println output
   124→cargo test spec_default       # Run specific test
   125→```
   126→
   127→## Project Structure
   128→
   129→```
   130→tspec/
   131→  src/
   132→    lib.rs          # Library root, exposes modules
   133→    main.rs         # Entry point, dispatch
   134→    cli.rs          # Clap CLI definitions
   135→    types.rs        # Spec parameter types
   136→    tspec.rs        # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   137→    find_paths.rs   # Project/crate/tspec/binary path discovery
   138→    workspace.rs    # Workspace member discovery
   139→    cargo_build.rs  # Build command + generated build.rs
   140→    run.rs          # Run command implementation
   141→    testing.rs      # Test command implementation
   142→    compare.rs      # Compare command (size comparison)
   143→    all.rs          # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all)
   144→    ts_cmd/         # Tspec management subcommands
   145→    binary.rs       # Binary operations (strip, size)
   146→  tests/
   147→    data/           # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   148→    tspec_test.rs   # Integration tests
   149→  notes/            # Design docs and todo tracking
   150→```

```
     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It allows configuring builds via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping.
     8→
     9→## Build Commands
    10→
    11→Ensure a recent tspec is installed:
    12→```bash
    13→cargo install --path .         # Install from local source
    14→```
    15→
    16→Development workflow:
    17→```bash
    18→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests
    19→tspec clean                    # Clean build artifacts
    20→tspec clippy                   # Run lints
    21→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting
    22→```
    23→
    24→## Architecture
    25→
    26→### Execute Trait Pattern
    27→
    28→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    29→
    30→```rust
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    37→```
    38→
    39→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    40→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    41→
    42→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    43→```rust
    44→enum Commands {
    45→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    46→    Test(TestCmd),
    47→    // ...
    48→}
    49→```
    50→
    51→### Key Modules
    52→
    53→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    54→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, execute_cargo_subcommand helper, re-exports
    55→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    56→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    57→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    58→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing
    59→- `cargo_build.rs` - Build orchestration with spec application
    60→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace member discovery
    61→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    62→
    63→### Translation Spec Structure
    64→
    65→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    66→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    67→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    68→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    69→
    70→## Conventions
    71→
    72→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    73→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    74→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-crate projects; tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    75→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    76→
    77→## Workflow
    78→
    79→**Before committing, run verification:**
    80→```bash
    81→tspec test -p tspec
    82→tspec clippy
    83→tspec fmt --check
    84→```
    85→
    86→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
    87→```
    88→Committed abc123.
    89→
    90→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
    91→```
    92→
    93→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
    94→
    95→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
    96→
    97→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
    98→
    99→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
   100→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   101→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   102→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   103→
   104→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   105→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

The main thing is adding a brief note in README.md that makes clear: **a tspec applies to a Cargo package** (the unit with a `Cargo.toml`), not to individual crates within a package.     23→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
    24→use std::ffi::OsString;
    25→use std::path::Path;
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;
    27→
    28→use crate::find_paths::get_crate_name;
    29→
    30→/// Trait for command execution.
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    37→    subcommand: &str,
    38→    args: &[OsString],
    39→    project_root: &Path,
    40→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    41→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    42→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    43→    cmd.args(args);
    44→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    45→    let status = cmd
    46→        .status()
    47→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    48→    if status.success() {
    49→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    50→    } else {

     1→//! `tspec ts hash` - Show tspec content hash
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, get_crate_name, resolve_package_dir};
     8→use crate::tspec::{hash_spec, load_spec};
     9→
    10→use super::list::find_tspec_files;
    11→
    12→/// Show hash of a tspec file
    13→pub fn hash_tspec(
    14→    project_root: &Path,
    15→    package: Option<&str>,
    16→    all: bool,
    17→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    18→) -> Result<()> {
    19→    let workspace = project_root;
    20→
    21→    // Check if we're in a package directory
    22→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    23→    let in_package_dir = get_crate_name(&cwd).is_ok();
    24→
    25→    // Resolve: --all > -p PKG > cwd > all
    26→    let hash_all = all || (package.is_none() && !in_package_dir);
    27→
    28→    if let Some(name) = package {
    29→        // Explicit package specified
    30→        let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, Some(name))?;
    31→        hash_package_tspecs(&package_dir, name, tspec)?;
    32→    } else if hash_all {
    33→        // Hash all packages
    34→        let info = crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    35→        for member in &info.members {
    36→            hash_package_tspecs(&member.path, &member.name, tspec)?;
    37→        }
    38→    } else {
    39→        // In a package directory
    40→        let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&cwd)?;
    41→        hash_package_tspecs(&cwd, &pkg_name, tspec)?;
    42→    }
    43→
    44→    Ok(())
    45→}
    46→
    47→/// Hash tspecs for a single package
    48→fn hash_package_tspecs(
    49→    package_dir: &std::path::Path,
    50→    pkg_name: &str,
    51→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    52→) -> Result<()> {
    53→    match tspec {
    54→        Some(name) => {
    55→            // Explicit tspec - hash just that one
    56→            let path = find_tspec(package_dir, Some(name))?;
    57→            match path {
    58→                Some(p) => print_tspec_hash(&p)?,
    59→                None => anyhow::bail!("tspec '{}' not found for package '{}'", name, pkg_name),
    60→            }
    61→        }
    62→        None => {
    63→            // No tspec specified - hash all tspec files
    64→            let tspecs = find_tspec_files(package_dir)?;
    65→            if tspecs.is_empty() {
    66→                println!("No *{} files found for {}", TSPEC_SUFFIX, pkg_name);
    67→            } else {
    68→                for name in &tspecs {
    69→                    print_tspec_hash(&package_dir.join(name))?;
    70→                }
    71→            }
    72→        }
    73→    }
    74→    Ok(())
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Print hash of a single tspec file
    78→fn print_tspec_hash(path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    79→    let filename = path
    80→        .file_name()
    81→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy())
    82→        .unwrap_or_default();
    83→    let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    84→    let hash = hash_spec(&spec)?;
    85→    println!("{}: {}", filename, hash);
    86→    Ok(())
    87→}
    88→

     1→//! `tspec ts show` - Show tspec contents
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, get_crate_name, resolve_package_dir};
     8→
     9→use super::list::find_tspec_files;
    10→
    11→/// Show a tspec file's contents
    12→pub fn show_tspec(
    13→    project_root: &Path,
    14→    package: Option<&str>,
    15→    all: bool,
    16→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    17→) -> Result<()> {
    18→    let workspace = project_root;
    19→
    20→    // Check if we're in a package directory
    21→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    22→    let in_package_dir = get_crate_name(&cwd).is_ok();
    23→
    24→    // Resolve: --all > -p PKG > cwd > all
    25→    let show_all = all || (package.is_none() && !in_package_dir);
    26→
    27→    if let Some(name) = package {
    28→        // Explicit package specified
    29→        let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, Some(name))?;
    30→        show_package_tspecs(&package_dir, name, tspec)?;
    31→    } else if show_all {
    32→        // Show all packages
    33→        let info = crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    34→        for (i, member) in info.members.iter().enumerate() {
    35→            if i > 0 {
    36→                println!();
    37→            }
    38→            println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
    39→            show_package_tspecs(&member.path, &member.name, tspec)?;
    40→        }
    41→    } else {
    42→        // In a package directory
    43→        let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&cwd)?;
    44→        show_package_tspecs(&cwd, &pkg_name, tspec)?;
    45→    }
    46→
    47→    Ok(())
    48→}
    49→
    50→/// Show tspecs for a single package
    51→fn show_package_tspecs(
    52→    package_dir: &std::path::Path,
    53→    pkg_name: &str,
    54→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    55→) -> Result<()> {
    56→    match tspec {
    57→        Some(name) => {
    58→            // Explicit tspec - show just that one
    59→            let path = find_tspec(package_dir, Some(name))?;
    60→            match path {
    61→                Some(p) => print_tspec_content(&p)?,
    62→                None => anyhow::bail!("tspec '{}' not found for package '{}'", name, pkg_name),
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        None => {
    66→            // No tspec specified - show all tspec files
    67→            let tspecs = find_tspec_files(package_dir)?;
    68→            if tspecs.is_empty() {
    69→                println!("No *{} files found for {}", TSPEC_SUFFIX, pkg_name);
    70→            } else {
    71→                for (i, name) in tspecs.iter().enumerate() {
    72→                    if i > 0 {
    73→                        println!();
    74→                    }
    75→                    print_tspec_content(&package_dir.join(name))?;
    76→                }
    77→            }
    78→        }
    79→    }
    80→    Ok(())
    81→}
    82→
    83→/// Print a single tspec file with header
    84→fn print_tspec_content(path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    85→    let filename = path
    86→        .file_name()
    87→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy())
    88→        .unwrap_or_default();
    89→    println!("====== {} ======", filename);
    90→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)?;
    91→    print!("{}", content);
    92→    if !content.ends_with('\n') {
    93→        println!();
    94→    }
    95→    Ok(())
    96→}
    97→

     1→//! `tspec ts list` - List tspec files
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{get_crate_name, resolve_package_dir};
     8→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;
     9→
    10→/// List all tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    11→pub fn list_tspecs(project_root: &Path, package: Option<&str>, all: bool) -> Result<()> {
    12→    let workspace = project_root;
    13→
    14→    // Check if we're in a package directory (has Cargo.toml with [package], not just workspace)
    15→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    16→    let in_package_dir = get_crate_name(&cwd).is_ok();
    17→
    18→    // Resolve: --all > -p PKG > cwd > all
    19→    let list_all = all || (package.is_none() && !in_package_dir);
    20→
    21→    if let Some(name) = package {
    22→        // Explicit package specified
    23→        let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, Some(name))?;
    24→        let tspecs = find_tspec_files(&package_dir)?;
    25→        print_package_tspecs(name, &package_dir, &tspecs);
    26→    } else if list_all {
    27→        // List all packages
    28→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
    29→        let mut found_any = false;
    30→
    31→        for member in &info.members {
    32→            let tspecs = find_tspec_files(&member.path)?;
    33→            if !tspecs.is_empty() {
    34→                print_package_tspecs(&member.name, &member.path, &tspecs);
    35→                found_any = true;
    36→            }
    37→        }
    38→
    39→        if !found_any {
    40→            println!("No *{} files found in workspace", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    41→        }
    42→    } else {
    43→        // In a package directory, list just this package
    44→        let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&cwd)?;
    45→        let tspecs = find_tspec_files(&cwd)?;
    46→        print_package_tspecs(&pkg_name, &cwd, &tspecs);
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    Ok(())
    50→}
    51→
    52→/// Find all tspec files (files ending with TSPEC_SUFFIX) in a directory
    53→pub(crate) fn find_tspec_files(dir: &Path) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    54→    let mut files = Vec::new();
    55→
    56→    if let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) {
    57→        for entry in entries.filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
    58→            let name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
    59→            if name.ends_with(TSPEC_SUFFIX) {
    60→                files.push(name);
    61→            }
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→
    65→    files.sort();
    66→    Ok(files)
    67→}
    68→
    69→/// Print tspec files for a package
    70→fn print_package_tspecs(package_name: &str, package_dir: &Path, tspecs: &[String]) {
    71→    println!("{}:", package_name);
    72→    for tspec in tspecs {
    73→        let path = package_dir.join(tspec);
    74→        let size = std::fs::metadata(&path)
    75→            .map(|m| format_size(m.len()))
    76→            .unwrap_or_else(|_| "?".to_string());
    77→        println!("  {} ({})", tspec, size);
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→
    81→/// Format file size in human-readable form
    82→pub(crate) fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
    83→    if bytes < 1024 {
    84→        format!("{} B", bytes)
    85→    } else {
    86→        format!("{:.1} KB", bytes as f64 / 1024.0)
    87→    }
    88→}
    89→
    90→#[cfg(test)]
    91→mod tests {
    92→    use super::*;
    93→    use crate::test_constants::SUFFIX;
    94→    use tempfile::TempDir;
    95→
    96→    #[test]
    97→    fn format_size_bytes() {
    98→        assert_eq!(format_size(100), "100 B");
    99→        assert_eq!(format_size(1023), "1023 B");
   100→    }
   101→
   102→    #[test]
   103→    fn format_size_kb() {
   104→        assert_eq!(format_size(1024), "1.0 KB");
   105→        assert_eq!(format_size(2560), "2.5 KB");
   106→    }
   107→
   108→    #[test]
   109→    fn find_tspec_files_empty_dir() {
   110→        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   111→        let files = find_tspec_files(dir.path()).unwrap();
   112→        assert!(files.is_empty());
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    #[test]
   116→    fn find_tspec_files_returns_sorted() {
   117→        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   118→
   119→        // Create files in non-alphabetical order

   121→        std::fs::write(dir.path().join(format!("alpha{}", SUFFIX)), "").unwrap();
   122→        std::fs::write(dir.path().join(format!("middle{}", SUFFIX)), "").unwrap();
   123→
   124→        let files = find_tspec_files(dir.path()).unwrap();

   128→        assert_eq!(files[1], format!("middle{}", SUFFIX));

   157→/// Find the tspec for a crate - tries as path first, then relative to crate_dir
   158→/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)
   159→pub fn find_tspec(crate_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {
   160→    match explicit {
   161→        Some(name) => {
   162→            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   163→            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   164→            if as_path.exists() {
   165→                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));
   166→            }
   167→
   168→            // Fallback: relative to crate directory
   169→            let in_crate = crate_dir.join(name);
   170→            if in_crate.exists() {
   171→                return Ok(Some(in_crate));
   172→            }
   173→
   174→            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension
   175→            if !name.contains('.') {
   176→                let with_suffix = crate_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   177→                if with_suffix.exists() {
   178→                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));
   179→                }
   180→            }
   181→
   182→            bail!("tspec not found: {}", name);
   183→        }
   184→        None => {
   185→            let default = crate_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   186→            if default.exists() {
   187→                Ok(Some(default))
   188→            } else {
   189→                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build
   190→            }
   191→        }
   192→    }
   193→}
   194→
   195→/// Find multiple tspecs by glob patterns
   196→/// If no patterns given, defaults to "tspec*{TSPEC_SUFFIX}"
   197→/// Returns sorted list of paths, errors if none found
   198→pub fn find_tspecs(crate_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {
   199→    let default_pattern = format!("tspec*{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   200→    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {
   201→        vec![&default_pattern]
   202→    } else {
   203→        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   204→    };
   205→
   206→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   207→
   208→    for pattern in &patterns {
   209→        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   210→        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   211→        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {
   212→            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   213→            continue;
   214→        }
   215→
   216→        // Try as literal path relative to crate_dir
   217→        let in_crate = crate_dir.join(pattern);
   218→        if in_crate.exists() && in_crate.is_file() {
   219→            results.push(in_crate);
   220→            continue;
   221→        }
   222→
   223→        // Try as glob pattern in crate_dir
   224→        let glob_pattern =
   225→            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!("invalid glob pattern: {}", pattern))?;
   226→
   227→        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(crate_dir)
   228→            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read directory: {}", crate_dir.display()))?
   229→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   230→            .filter(|e| {
   231→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   232→                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)
   233→            })
   234→            .map(|e| e.path())
   235→            .collect();
   236→
   237→        results.extend(entries);
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    // Remove duplicates and sort
   241→    results.sort();
   242→    results.dedup();
   243→
   244→    if results.is_empty() {
   245→        let pattern_list = patterns.join(", ");
   246→        bail!(
   247→            "no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}",
   248→            pattern_list,
   249→            crate_dir.display()
   250→        );
   251→    }
   252→
   253→    Ok(results)
   254→}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "/// Find the tspec for a crate - tries as path first, then relative to crate_dir\n/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)\npub fn find_tspec(crate_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {\n    match explicit {\n        Some(name) => {\n            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)\n            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);\n            if as_path.exists() {\n                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));\n            }\n\n            // Fallback: relative to crate directory\n            let in_crate = crate_dir.join(name);\n            if in_crate.exists() {\n                return Ok(Some(in_crate));\n            }\n\n            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension\n            if !name.contains('.') {\n                let with_suffix = crate_dir.join(format!(\"{}{}\", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));\n                if with_suffix.exists() {\n                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));\n                }\n            }\n\n            bail!(\"tspec not found: {}\", name);\n        }\n        None => {\n            let default = crate_dir.join(format!(\"tspec{}\", TSPEC_SUFFIX));\n            if default.exists() {\n                Ok(Some(default))\n            } else {\n                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Find the tspec for a package - tries as path first, then relative to pkg_dir\n/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)\npub fn find_tspec(pkg_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {\n    match explicit {\n        Some(name) => {\n            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)\n            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);\n            if as_path.exists() {\n                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));\n            }\n\n            // Fallback: relative to package directory\n            let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(name);\n            if in_pkg.exists() {\n                return Ok(Some(in_pkg));\n            }\n\n            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension\n            if !name.contains('.') {\n                let with_suffix = pkg_dir.join(format!(\"{}{}\", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));\n                if with_suffix.exists() {\n                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));\n                }\n            }\n\n            bail!(\"tspec not found: {}\", name);\n        }\n        None => {\n            let default = pkg_dir.join(format!(\"tspec{}\", TSPEC_SUFFIX));\n            if default.exists() {\n                Ok(Some(default))\n            } else {\n                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "pub fn find_tspecs(crate_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {\n    let default_pattern = format!(\"tspec*{}\", TSPEC_SUFFIX);\n    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {\n        vec![&default_pattern]\n    } else {\n        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()\n    };\n\n    let mut results = Vec::new();\n\n    for pattern in &patterns {\n        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)\n        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);\n        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {\n            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Try as literal path relative to crate_dir\n        let in_crate = crate_dir.join(pattern);\n        if in_crate.exists() && in_crate.is_file() {\n            results.push(in_crate);\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Try as glob pattern in crate_dir\n        let glob_pattern =\n            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!(\"invalid glob pattern: {}\", pattern))?;\n\n        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(crate_dir)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"cannot read directory: {}\", crate_dir.display()))?\n            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())\n            .filter(|e| {\n                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();\n                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)\n            })\n            .map(|e| e.path())\n            .collect();\n\n        results.extend(entries);\n    }\n\n    // Remove duplicates and sort\n    results.sort();\n    results.dedup();\n\n    if results.is_empty() {\n        let pattern_list = patterns.join(\", \");\n        bail!(\n            \"no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}\",\n            pattern_list,\n            crate_dir.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    Ok(results)\n}",
  "new_string": "pub fn find_tspecs(pkg_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {\n    let default_pattern = format!(\"tspec*{}\", TSPEC_SUFFIX);\n    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {\n        vec![&default_pattern]\n    } else {\n        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()\n    };\n\n    let mut results = Vec::new();\n\n    for pattern in &patterns {\n        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)\n        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);\n        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {\n            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Try as literal path relative to pkg_dir\n        let in_pkg = pkg_dir.join(pattern);\n        if in_pkg.exists() && in_pkg.is_file() {\n            results.push(in_pkg);\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Try as glob pattern in pkg_dir\n        let glob_pattern =\n            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!(\"invalid glob pattern: {}\", pattern))?;\n\n        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(pkg_dir)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"cannot read directory: {}\", pkg_dir.display()))?\n            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())\n            .filter(|e| {\n                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();\n                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)\n            })\n            .map(|e| e.path())\n            .collect();\n\n        results.extend(entries);\n    }\n\n    // Remove duplicates and sort\n    results.sort();\n    results.dedup();\n\n    if results.is_empty() {\n        let pattern_list = patterns.join(\", \");\n        bail!(\n            \"no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}\",\n            pattern_list,\n            pkg_dir.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    Ok(results)\n}"
}

   106→
   107→/// Classify a package based on its path and name
   108→fn classify_package(path: &Path, name: &str) -> PackageKind {
   109→    let path_str = path.to_string_lossy();
   110→
   111→    // Build tools at workspace root level
   112→    if name == "tspec" || name == "xt" || name == "xtask" {
   113→        return PackageKind::BuildTool;
   114→    }
   115→
   116→    // Test crates
   117→    if path_str.contains("/tests") || name.ends_with("-tests") {
   118→        return PackageKind::Test;
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    // Categorize by directory
   122→    if path_str.contains("apps/") {
   123→        PackageKind::App
   124→    } else if path_str.contains("libs/") {
   125→        PackageKind::Lib
   126→    } else if path_str.contains("tools/") {
   127→        PackageKind::Tool
   128→    } else {
   129→        // Unknown location, treat as build tool (excluded)
   130→        PackageKind::BuildTool
   131→    }
   132→}
   133→
   134→#[cfg(test)]
   135→mod tests {
   136→    use super::*;
   137→
   138→    #[test]
   139→    fn classify_app() {
   140→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/apps/ex-x1");
   141→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "ex-x1"), PackageKind::App);
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    #[test]
   145→    fn classify_lib() {
   146→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/libs/rlibc-x1");
   147→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "rlibc-x1"), PackageKind::Lib);
   148→    }
   149→
   150→    #[test]
   151→    fn classify_tool() {
   152→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/tools/is-libc-used");
   153→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "is-libc-used"), PackageKind::Tool);
   154→    }
   155→
   156→    #[test]
   157→    fn classify_test_by_path() {
   158→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/libs/rlibc-x2/tests");
   159→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "rlibc-x2-tests"), PackageKind::Test);
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    #[test]
   163→    fn classify_test_by_name() {
   164→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/somewhere");
   165→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "foo-tests"), PackageKind::Test);
   166→    }
   167→
   168→    #[test]
   169→    fn classify_build_tool_xt() {
   170→        let path = PathBuf::from("/workspace/xt");
   171→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "xt"), PackageKind::BuildTool);
   172→    }
   173→
   174→    #[test]
   175→    fn classify_build_tool_xtask() {

   177→        assert_eq!(classify_package(&path, "xtask"), PackageKind::BuildTool);
   178→    }

   180→    #[test]
   181→    fn discover_works() {
   182→        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs
   183→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();
   184→        if let Ok(info) = info {

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Usage
    19→
    20→```bash
    21→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    22→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    23→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    24→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    25→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbol with tspec.ts.toml if present
    26→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    27→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    28→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    29→tspec compare -p myapp -r                  # Compare all tspec*.ts.toml by binary size
    30→```
    31→
    32→The `-p` flag specifies a package (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages).
    33→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    34→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically.
    35→
    36→## tspec Files
    37→
    38→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    39→
    40→```toml
    41→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    42→
    43→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    44→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    45→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    46→
    47→[cargo]
    48→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release"
    49→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    50→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (mutually exclusive with target_triple)
    51→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    52→target_dir = "<name>"                      # per-spec target dir; supports <name> and <hash> placeholders
    53→
    54→[rustc]
    55→opt_level = "z"                            # "0", "1", "2", "3", "s", "z"
    56→panic = "abort"                            # "abort", "unwind", "immediate-abort" (prefer top-level panic)
    57→lto = true
    58→codegen_units = 1
    59→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    60→flags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw rustc flags passed through
    61→
    62→[linker]
    63→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
    64→
    65→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
    66→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
    67→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
    68→```
    69→
    70→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically. If both
    71→top-level `panic` and `rustc.panic` are set, both are applied (prefer one or the other).
    72→
    73→### Key Concepts
    74→
    75→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
    76→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
    77→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs
    78→
    79→### How Linker Flags Work
    80→
    81→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
    82→
    83→```rust
    84→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
    85→fn main() {
    86→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
    87→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
    88→}
    89→```
    90→
    91→This is necessary because RUSTFLAGS affects ALL compilations (including dependency
    92→build scripts), causing crashes with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated
    93→build.rs scopes flags to just the binary.
    94→
    95→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
    96→
    97→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing:
    98→
    99→```bash
   100→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   101→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List all tspec files for a package
   102→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents, i.e. *.ts.toml
   103→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   104→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   105→tspec ts hash -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show content hash of tspec-opt.ts.toml
   106→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml
   107→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   108→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec creating opt2.ts.toml
   109→tspec ts set strip=symbols -p myapp      # Set value in tspec.ts.toml
   110→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Create versioned backup (name-NNN-hash.ts.toml)
   111→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup myapp/tspec-opt.ts.toml to myapp/tspec-opt-<next-seqnum>-<hash>.ts.toml
   112→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup t1-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   113→```
   114→
   115→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   116→
   117→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   118→
   119→## Testing
   120→
   121→```bash
   122→cargo test                    # Run all tests
   123→cargo test -- --nocapture     # See println output
   124→cargo test spec_default       # Run specific test
   125→```
   126→
   127→## Project Structure
   128→
   129→```
   130→tspec/
   131→  src/
   132→    lib.rs          # Library root, exposes modules
   133→    main.rs         # Entry point, dispatch
   134→    cli.rs          # Clap CLI definitions
   135→    types.rs        # Spec parameter types
   136→    tspec.rs        # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   137→    find_paths.rs   # Project/crate/tspec/binary path discovery
   138→    workspace.rs    # Workspace member discovery
   139→    cargo_build.rs  # Build command + generated build.rs
   140→    run.rs          # Run command implementation
   141→    testing.rs      # Test command implementation
   142→    compare.rs      # Compare command (size comparison)
   143→    all.rs          # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all)
   144→    ts_cmd/         # Tspec management subcommands
   145→    binary.rs       # Binary operations (strip, size)
   146→  tests/
   147→    data/           # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   148→    tspec_test.rs   # Integration tests
   149→  notes/            # Design docs and todo tracking
   150→```
   151→
   152→## Development
   153→
   154→See [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md) for current tasks.
   155→
   156→### Verification
   157→
   158→```bash
   159→cargo test
   160→cargo clippy --all-targets
   161→cargo fmt --check
   162→```
   163→
   164→## Origin
   165→
   166→Originally developed as `xt` within the [rlibc-x](https://github.com/user/rlibc-x) project, then extracted to a standalone tool. The git history was preserved using `git subtree split`.
   167→
   168→## License
   169→
   170→Licensed under either of
   171→
   172→- Apache License, Version 2.0 ([LICENSE-APACHE](LICENSE-APACHE) or http://apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0)
   173→- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
   174→
   175→### Contribution
   176→
   177→Unless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted
   178→for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
   179→be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
   180→
