```
     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It allows configuring builds via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping.
     8→
     9→## Build Commands
    10→
    11→Ensure a recent tspec is installed:
    12→```bash
    13→cargo install --path .         # Install from local source
    14→```
    15→
    16→Development workflow:
    17→```bash
    18→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests
    19→tspec clean                    # Clean build artifacts
    20→tspec clippy                   # Run lints
    21→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting
    22→```
    23→
    24→## Architecture
    25→
    26→### Execute Trait Pattern
    27→
    28→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    29→
    30→```rust
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    37→```
    38→
    39→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    40→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    41→
    42→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    43→```rust
    44→enum Commands {
    45→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    46→    Test(TestCmd),
    47→    // ...
    48→}
    49→```
    50→
    51→### Key Modules
    52→
    53→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    54→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, execute_cargo_subcommand helper, re-exports
    55→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    56→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    57→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    58→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing
    59→- `cargo_build.rs` - Build orchestration with spec application
    60→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace member discovery
    61→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    62→
    63→### Translation Spec Structure
    64→
    65→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    66→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    67→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    68→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    69→
    70→## Conventions
    71→
    72→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    73→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    74→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-crate projects; tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    75→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    76→
    77→## Workflow
    78→
    79→**Before committing, run verification:**
    80→```bash
    81→tspec test -p tspec
    82→tspec clippy
    83→tspec fmt --check
    84→```
    85→
    86→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
    87→```
    88→Committed abc123.
    89→
    90→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
    91→```
    92→
    93→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
    94→
    95→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
    96→
    97→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
    98→
    99→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
   100→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   101→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   102→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   103→
   104→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   105→

<system-reminder>

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

     1→//! `tspec ts set` - Set a scalar value in a tspec
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     7→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     8→use crate::tspec::{load_spec, save_spec};
     9→use crate::types::{OptLevel, PanicStrategy, Profile, Spec};
    10→
    11→/// Set a value in a tspec and save in place
    12→pub fn set_value(
    13→    project_root: &Path,
    14→    package: Option<&str>,
    15→    key: &str,
    16→    value: &str,
    17→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    18→) -> Result<()> {
    19→    let workspace = project_root;
    20→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    21→
    22→    // Load existing spec and its path, or use default with constructed path
    23→    let (mut spec, output_path) = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    24→        Some(path) => (load_spec(&path)?, path),
    25→        None => {
    26→            let base_name = match tspec {
    27→                Some(t) => t
    28→                    .strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    29→                    .or_else(|| t.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    30→                    .unwrap_or(t),
    31→                None => "tspec",
    32→            };
    33→            let path = package_dir.join(format!("{}{}", base_name, crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX));
    34→            (Spec::default(), path)
    35→        }
    36→    };
    37→
    38→    // Apply the key=value change
    39→    apply_value(&mut spec, key, value)?;
    40→
    41→    // Save in place
    42→    save_spec(&spec, &output_path)?;
    43→
    44→    println!(
    45→        "Saved {}",
    46→        output_path
    47→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    48→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    49→            .display()
    50→    );
    51→
    52→    Ok(())
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Apply key=value to a spec
    56→fn apply_value(spec: &mut Spec, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    57→    match key {
    58→        // Top-level high-level options
    59→        "panic" => {
    60→            spec.panic = Some(parse_panic_mode(value)?);
    61→        }
    62→        "strip" => {
    63→            spec.strip = Some(parse_strip_mode(value)?);
    64→        }
    65→
    66→        // Cargo config
    67→        "cargo.profile" => {
    68→            spec.cargo.profile = Some(parse_profile(value)?);
    69→        }
    70→        "cargo.target_triple" => {
    71→            spec.cargo.target_triple = Some(value.to_string());
    72→        }
    73→        "cargo.target_dir" => {
    74→            spec.cargo.target_dir = Some(value.to_string());
    75→        }
    76→
    77→        // Rustc config
    78→        "rustc.opt_level" => {
    79→            spec.rustc.opt_level = Some(parse_opt_level(value)?);
    80→        }
    81→        "rustc.panic" => {
    82→            spec.rustc.panic = Some(parse_panic_strategy(value)?);
    83→        }
    84→        "rustc.lto" => {
    85→            spec.rustc.lto = Some(parse_bool(value)?);
    86→        }
    87→        "rustc.codegen_units" => {
    88→            spec.rustc.codegen_units = Some(
    89→                value
    90→                    .parse()
    91→                    .with_context(|| format!("invalid codegen_units: {}", value))?,
    92→            );
    93→        }
    94→
    95→        _ => bail!("unknown key: {}", key),
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    Ok(())
    99→}
   100→
   101→fn parse_panic_mode(s: &str) -> Result<PanicMode> {
   102→    match s {
   103→        "unwind" => Ok(PanicMode::Unwind),
   104→        "abort" => Ok(PanicMode::Abort),
   105→        "immediate-abort" => Ok(PanicMode::ImmediateAbort),
   106→        _ => bail!(
   107→            "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
   108→            s
   109→        ),
   110→    }
   111→}
   112→
   113→fn parse_strip_mode(s: &str) -> Result<StripMode> {
   114→    match s {
   115→        "none" => Ok(StripMode::None),
   116→        "debuginfo" => Ok(StripMode::Debuginfo),
   117→        "symbols" => Ok(StripMode::Symbols),
   118→        _ => bail!(
   119→            "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
   120→            s
   121→        ),
   122→    }
   123→}
   124→
   125→fn parse_profile(s: &str) -> Result<Profile> {
   126→    match s {
   127→        "debug" => Ok(Profile::Debug),
   128→        "release" => Ok(Profile::Release),
   129→        _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", s),
   130→    }
   131→}
   132→
   133→fn parse_opt_level(s: &str) -> Result<OptLevel> {
   134→    match s {
   135→        "0" => Ok(OptLevel::O0),
   136→        "1" => Ok(OptLevel::O1),
   137→        "2" => Ok(OptLevel::O2),
   138→        "3" => Ok(OptLevel::O3),
   139→        "s" => Ok(OptLevel::Os),
   140→        "z" => Ok(OptLevel::Oz),
   141→        _ => bail!("invalid opt-level: {} (expected: 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z)", s),
   142→    }
   143→}
   144→
   145→fn parse_panic_strategy(s: &str) -> Result<PanicStrategy> {
   146→    match s {
   147→        "abort" => Ok(PanicStrategy::Abort),
   148→        "unwind" => Ok(PanicStrategy::Unwind),
   149→        "immediate-abort" => Ok(PanicStrategy::ImmediateAbort),
   150→        _ => bail!(
   151→            "invalid panic strategy: {} (expected: abort, unwind, immediate-abort)",
   152→            s
   153→        ),
   154→    }
   155→}
   156→
   157→fn parse_bool(s: &str) -> Result<bool> {
   158→    match s {
   159→        "true" | "yes" | "1" => Ok(true),
   160→        "false" | "no" | "0" => Ok(false),
   161→        _ => bail!("invalid boolean: {} (expected: true/false)", s),
   162→    }
   163→}

> AGENT

Good list. Here are my thoughts on each:

**1. `classify_crate` (now `classify_package`) is brittle** — Still true. It uses hardcoded path patterns (`apps/`, `libs/`, `tools/`) and names (`tspec`, `xt`, `xtask`). This only matters for multi-package workspaces though, and yours have that convention. A more robust approach could read `Cargo.toml` metadata (e.g. `[package.metadata.tspec] kind = "app"`) or just check whether the package has binary targets. Low priority unless you start using tspec with workspaces that don't follow that layout.

**2. `-p` shouldn't be needed for `ts compare` in a POP** — Straightforward. The other commands (build, run, test) already default to the current directory. `compare` should too.

**3. `-t` should support glob like in compare** — Makes sense. `tspec build -t "tspec*.ts.toml"` to build with each matching spec sequentially. The `find_tspecs` infrastructure already exists from compare.

**4. Add `ts unset` or `ts remove`** — Useful. Currently you can set values but can't clear them back to None. Something like `tspec ts unset rustc.lto` to remove a field. Simple to implement — set the field to `None` and re-serialize.

**5. How to add/remove from arrays** — This is the harder version of #4. Fields like `linker.args`, `rustc.flags`, `rustc.build_std`, `cargo.unstable` are all `Vec<String>`. You'd need syntax like `tspec ts set linker.args+="-static"` and `tspec ts set linker.args-="-static"`. Or separate `ts add`/`ts remove` subcommands. This is where `ts set` starts becoming a mini TOML editor.

**6. `ts set` doesn't handle array/nested fields** — This is the immediate bug. `ts set` only handles scalars via that hardcoded `match` in `apply_value`. It's missing: `rustc.build_std`, `rustc.flags`, `cargo.unstable`, `linker.args`, `cargo.target_json`, and `linker.version_script`. Items #4, #5, and #6 are really the same problem — `ts set` is incomplete.

My suggestion on priority: #6 is the most impactful since it's a broken expectation (fields documented in README but not settable). #2 is a quick win. #4 pairs naturally with #6. #3 and #5 are nice-to-haves. #1 only matters when you take tspec to other workspaces.

What's your thinking on priority?

> AGENT

Good news: `toml_edit` v0.22.27 is already a transitive dependency through `toml`. Adding it as a direct dependency adds zero new code to compile.

Here's how I'd think about it:

**Current approach (serde round-trip):** `load_spec` → deserialize to `Spec` struct → modify struct → `save_spec` → serialize back. This loses all comments, blank lines, and custom formatting. Fine for `ts new`, `ts backup`, `ts restore` where canonical form is expected.

**toml_edit approach:** Parse into a `Document` (preserves everything), surgically edit just the key you touched, write back. Comments, blank lines, ordering — all preserved.

The two approaches can coexist:
- **serde path** (`toml`): Keep for `load_spec`/`save_spec`/`hash_spec`/`save_spec_snapshot` — these need canonical form for hashing and clean output
- **toml_edit path**: Use for `ts set`, `ts unset`, and array add/remove — these are surgical edits where preserving the user's file is important

This naturally solves #4, #5, and #6 together:
- **set scalar**: `doc["rustc"]["lto"] = value(true)`
- **unset**: `doc["rustc"].as_table_mut().remove("lto")`
- **array add**: `doc["linker"]["args"].as_array_mut().push("--static")`
- **array remove**: find and remove from the array

The `apply_value` match statement goes away entirely — `toml_edit` can handle any key path generically since it operates on the document tree, not a hardcoded struct. So `rustc.build_std`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable` — they all just work without adding new match arms.

Want me to plan this out?

> DEVELOPER

You say use "serde path (toml)" for load and hash, that seems fine but for writing saving or editing that's not going to work. A `ts backup/restore` definitely need to preserve user info. But maybe I misunderstand

> AGENT

You're right, I was sloppy. Let me reconsider.

`ts backup` today does: `load_spec` (serde deserialize) → `save_spec_snapshot` (serde serialize). That round-trips through the struct and destroys comments. Same for `ts restore`. That's wrong — a backup should be an exact copy of what the user wrote.

The real split should be:

- **serde (`toml`)**: For loading specs into typed `Spec` structs — building, testing, hashing, comparison. Anything that needs to *understand* the spec. Also fine for `ts new` when creating a fresh spec from scratch (no user content to preserve).
- **Raw file copy**: For `ts backup` and `ts restore`. They don't modify content, they just copy files. Compute the hash from the serde path (for the filename), but write the original bytes.
- **toml_edit**: For `ts set`, `ts unset`, array add/remove. Surgical edits that preserve everything the user wrote.

So three strategies, not two. And the serde path should **never** be used to write back a file that the user authored.

> DEVELOPER

## Summary of tspec Commands: Comment/Formatting Preservation Analysis

Based on my exploration of the codebase, here's a detailed analysis of how each command works and whether they preserve user comments/formatting:

### 1. **`ts set` (in `/src/ts_cmd/set.rs`)**

**How it works:**
- Loads a spec from file via `load_spec()` which deserializes TOML → Rust struct
- Calls `apply_value()` to modify specific nested fields in the Spec struct
- Saves back via `save_spec()` which serializes Spec struct → TOML

**Supported keys:**
- High-level: `panic`, `strip`
- Cargo: `cargo.profile`, `cargo.target_triple`, `cargo.target_dir`
- Rustc: `rustc.opt_level`, `rustc.panic`, `rustc.lto`, `rustc.codegen_units`

**Comment/Formatting Preservation:** ❌ **LOST**
- Uses `toml::from_str()` to deserialize (generic serde)
- Uses `toml::to_string()` to serialize (loses all formatting)
- This is a **destructive round-trip**: any comments, blank lines, custom formatting vanishes

---

### 2. **`ts backup` (in `/src/ts_cmd/backup.rs`)**

**How it works:**
- Loads spec via `load_spec()` (deserialize TOML → struct)
- Calls `save_spec_snapshot()` which saves with versioned filename: `{name}-{seq:03d}-{hash}.ts.toml`
- Each backup gets an incrementing sequence number + 8-char SHA256 hash of the spec content

**Comment/Formatting Preservation:** ❌ **LOST**
- Uses same load/serialize round-trip as `ts set`
- Creates timestamped snapshots via serde, not file copying
- The file copy happens **through serde serialization**, not byte-for-byte copying

---

### 3. **`ts restore` (in `/src/ts_cmd/restore.rs`)**

**How it works:**
- Takes a backup filename like `t1-001-abcd1234.ts.toml`
- Parses it to extract base name via regex pattern: `{name}-NNN-HHHHHHHH`
- Loads backup spec via `load_spec()`
- Saves to target path using `save_spec()`: effectively copies spec content through serde

**Comment/Formatting Preservation:** ❌ **LOST**
- Same load/serialize flow as backup
- Restores the **serialized data**, not the original file bytes

---

### 4. **Core serialization in `/src/tspec.rs`**

**Key functions:**

| Function | Purpose | Comment Preservation |
|----------|---------|-----|
| `load_spec(path)` | Read TOML file, deserialize to Spec struct | ❌ Loses comments |
| `parse_spec(toml_str)` | Deserialize TOML string → struct | ❌ Loses comments |
| `serialize_spec(spec)` | Serialize struct → TOML string (canonical) | ❌ No comments |
| `save_spec(spec, path)` | Save spec to file (calls serialize_spec) | ❌ No comments |
| `save_spec_snapshot(spec, name, dir)` | Create versioned backup file | ❌ No comments |
| `hash_spec(spec)` | SHA256 hash first 4 bytes of serialized TOML | ✅ Stable |

**Key detail:** `serialize_spec()` uses `toml::to_string(spec)`, which produces **canonical form** (used for hashing). This is deterministic but loses all formatting.

---

### 5. **`ts new` (in `/src/ts_cmd/new.rs`)**

**How it works:**
- Creates a new spec file with optional `-f/--from` source
- If `--from` specified:
  - Resolves source spec name (can be `package/spec` or just `spec`)
  - Loads source spec via `load_spec()`
  - Clones the Spec struct: `source_spec.cloned().unwrap_or_default()`
  - Saves to new file via `save_spec()`
- If no source, creates empty default spec

**Comment/Formatting Preservation:** ❌ **LOST**
- Uses struct cloning, not file copying
- Destination is always serialized from scratch

---

### 6. **Dependency Versions**

**From Cargo.toml/Cargo.lock:**
- `toml = "0.8"` (serde-based deserializer/serializer)
- `toml_edit = "0.22.27"` (dependency via cargo metadata, not explicitly in Cargo.toml)

**Important:** `toml_edit` is available but **NOT currently used** in tspec. The `DocumentMut` API from `toml_edit` would allow comment-preserving edits, but tspec doesn't use it.

---

### 7. **Why Comments Are Lost**

The fundamental issue is the **serde round-trip**:

```
File (with comments)
    ↓ [load_spec]
Spec struct (data only)
    ↓ [apply_value or clone]
Modified Spec struct
    ↓ [serialize_spec via toml::to_string]
File (canonical form, no comments)
```

**All three commands** (`set`, `backup`, `restore`) follow this pattern. The serde library only captures the **data**, not metadata like comments or formatting.

---

### 8. **Preserved vs. Lossy**

| Operation | Comments | Blank Lines | Field Order | Formatting |
|-----------|----------|-------------|-------------|-----------|
| `ts set` | ❌ Lost | ❌ Lost | ❌ Reordered | ❌ Canonical |
| `ts backup` | ❌ Lost | ❌ Lost | ❌ Reordered | ❌ Canonical |
| `ts restore` | ❌ Lost | ❌ Lost | ❌ Reordered | ❌ Canonical |
| `ts new -f` | ❌ Lost | ❌ Lost | ❌ Reordered | ❌ Canonical |

**Show function** (`ts show`): Reads raw file via `std::fs::read_to_string()` - shows original content with comments intact (but doesn't modify).

---

### Conclusion

To preserve comments, you would need to:
1. **Replace serde-based operations** with `toml_edit::DocumentMut` (already in Cargo.lock)
2. Use **in-place table mutation** instead of deserialize→modify→serialize
3. Update `save_spec()` to write the `Document` directly instead of serializing struct

The `toml_edit` crate version 0.22.27 supports this via:
- `DocumentMut::parse()` - parse with comments
- `.get_mut("key")` - in-place edits
- `.to_string()` - write back preserving structure

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need to provide a comprehensive answer. Let me compile my findings:

## toml_edit 0.24.x API Reference

Based on my research, here's a comprehensive guide to using `toml_edit` (version 0.24.x, which is the latest version - note that 0.22.x has been superseded). The API has remained quite stable across recent versions.

### 1. Parsing TOML into DocumentMut

```rust
use toml_edit::DocumentMut;

// Parse from a string
let toml_str = r#"
[cargo]
profile = "release"

[rustc]
lto = true
"#;

let mut doc = toml_str.parse::<DocumentMut>()
    .expect("failed to parse TOML");
```

### 2. Setting Scalar Values at Nested Paths

```rust
use toml_edit::value;

// Set nested values using bracket notation
doc["rustc"]["lto"] = value(true);
doc["rustc"]["opt_level"] = value("3");
doc["cargo"]["profile"] = value("release");
doc["cargo"]["target_triple"] = value("x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu");

// Multiple levels deep
doc["section"]["subsection"]["key"] = value("hello");
```

The `value()` function is a helper that:
- Accepts any type implementing `Into<Value>`
- Properly formats the assignment with appropriate spacing
- Works with strings, numbers, booleans, arrays, etc.

**API Signature:**
```rust
pub fn value<V: Into<Value>>(v: V) -> Item
```

### 3. Unsetting/Removing Keys from a Table

```rust
// Remove a single key
doc["rustc"].as_table_mut().unwrap().remove("lto");

// Remove from nested tables
if let Some(table) = doc["rustc"].as_table_mut() {
    table.remove("codegen_units");
}

// Direct removal if you access a value
let removed = doc["cargo"].as_table_mut()
    .and_then(|t| t.remove("target_dir"));

// Remove all entries from a table
doc["rustc"].as_table_mut().unwrap().clear();
```

**Key Methods:**
- `remove(key: &str)` - Returns the removed item, if present
- `remove_entry(key: &str)` - Returns both key and item
- `clear()` - Remove all entries
- `retain(predicate)` - Keep only items matching a condition

### 4. Working with Arrays (linker.args, etc.)

```rust
use toml_edit::array;

// Create an empty array and populate it
let mut arr = array();
arr.push("value1");
arr.push("value2");
arr.push(42);
doc["linker"]["args"] = arr;

// Or add to existing array
if let Some(item) = doc["linker"]["args"].as_array_mut() {
    item.push("-static");
    item.push("-L/custom/path");
}

// Remove from array
if let Some(arr) = doc["linker"]["args"].as_array_mut() {
    arr.remove(0);  // Remove first element
}

// Iterate and modify
if let Some(arr) = doc["linker"]["args"].as_array_mut() {
    for value in arr.iter_mut() {
        // Modify each value
    }
}
```

**Array Methods:**
- `push(v)` - Append a value with default formatting
- `push_formatted(v)` - Append a pre-formatted Value
- `remove(index)` - Remove element at index
- `clear()` - Remove all elements
- `retain(predicate)` - Keep only items where predicate is true
- `iter()` / `iter_mut()` - Iterate over values
- `get(index)` / `get_mut(index)` - Access by index

**Helper Function:**
```rust
pub fn array() -> Item  // Returns an empty array
```

### 5. Writing Document Back to File

```rust
use std::fs;

// Convert to string (preserves all formatting/comments)
let updated_toml = doc.to_string();

// Write to file
fs::write("path/to/tspec.ts.toml", &updated_toml)
    .expect("failed to write file");
```

**Note:** The `DocumentMut` implements `Display` trait (when the `display` feature is enabled, which is typical), so:
- `.to_string()` preserves original formatting, comments, and spacing
- This is what makes `toml_edit` special — unlike `serde` which loses formatting

### 6. Creating Tables

```rust
use toml_edit::table;

// Create an empty table
let mut new_table = table();

// Add to document
doc["new_section"] = new_table;

// Control where new tables appear
if let Some(t) = doc["new_section"].as_table_mut() {
    t.set_position(1);  // Place after first element
}
```

**Helper Function:**
```rust
pub fn table() -> Item  // Returns an empty table
```

### 7. Complete Example: Simulating tspec's `ts set` Command

```rust
use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
use std::fs;

fn set_rustc_value(path: &str, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    let toml_str = fs::read_to_string(path)?;
    let mut doc = toml_str.parse::<DocumentMut>()?;
    
    // Ensure rustc section exists
    if !doc.contains_key("rustc") {
        doc["rustc"] = toml_edit::table();
    }
    
    // Set the value
    match key {
        "lto" => {
            let bool_val = value == "true";
            doc["rustc"]["lto"] = toml_edit::value(bool_val);
        }
        "opt_level" => {
            doc["rustc"]["opt_level"] = toml_edit::value(value);
        }
        "panic" => {
            doc["rustc"]["panic"] = toml_edit::value(value);
        }
        _ => return Err("unknown key".into()),
    }
    
    // Write back, preserving formatting
    fs::write(path, doc.to_string())?;
    Ok(())
}
```

---

## Dual Parsing: Serde + toml_edit

**Yes, you can absolutely parse the same TOML file twice — once into a serde struct and once into a DocumentMut:**

### Option 1: Parse from string twice

```rust
use serde::Deserialize;
use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
use std::fs;

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct Spec {
    cargo: Option<Cargo>,
    rustc: Option<Rustc>,
}

let toml_str = fs::read_to_string("tspec.ts.toml")?;

// Parse 1: Into serde struct
let spec: Spec = toml::from_str(&toml_str)?;

// Parse 2: Into DocumentMut for editing with formatting preservation
let mut doc = toml_str.parse::<DocumentMut>()?;

// Now you have both the typed struct AND the document for editing
```

### Option 2: Parse DocumentMut, then deserialize from it

```rust
use toml_edit::de;

let toml_str = fs::read_to_string("tspec.ts.toml")?;
let mut doc = toml_str.parse::<DocumentMut>()?;

// Deserialize serde struct from the document
let spec: Spec = de::from_document(doc.clone())?;

// Or deserialize specific sections
let rustc = de::from_document(doc["rustc"].clone())?;
```

**Key Functions in toml_edit::de:**
- `from_str(s: &str)` - Deserialize from string
- `from_slice(bytes: &[u8])` - Deserialize from bytes
- `from_document(d: DocumentMut)` - Deserialize from DocumentMut (requires `serde` feature)

**Note:** This requires enabling the `serde` feature in Cargo.toml:
```toml
toml_edit = { version = "0.24", features = ["serde"] }
```

---

## toml vs toml_edit Comparison

The current tspec project uses `toml = "0.8"` (serde-only). Here's when to consider switching:

| Aspect | `toml` crate | `toml_edit` crate |
|--------|---|---|
| **Use case** | Deserialize TOML into typed structs | Edit TOML while preserving formatting/comments |
| **Speed** | ~2x faster (~62μs parse time) | ~110μs parse time |
| **Preserves formatting** | No | Yes |
| **Preserves comments** | No | Yes |
| **Preserves item order** | No | Yes |
| **Serde support** | Yes (native) | Yes (optional feature) |
| **API complexity** | Simple | More complex |
| **Best for** | Config loading | Config file mutation/editing |

**Future note:** The `toml` crate now uses `toml_edit` internally for better TOML 1.0 compliance, with a simpler API layer on top.

---

## For tspec's `ts set` Command

Currently, tspec deserializes into the `Spec` struct, modifies it in memory, and writes it back via `toml::to_string()`. If you wanted to preserve user comments and formatting in `.ts.toml` files, you'd need to:

1. Switch to `toml_edit` for the `ts set` operation
2. Keep serde parsing for normal build operations
3. Use the dual-parse pattern shown above

This would be a significant change but would give users the benefit of keeping their manually-added comments intact when using `tspec ts set`.

Sources:
- [toml_edit on docs.rs](https://docs.rs/toml_edit/latest/toml_edit/)
- [toml vs toml_edit comparison](https://epage.github.io/blog/2023/01/toml-vs-toml-edit/)
- [Rust Forum discussion on toml_edit table insertion](https://users.rust-lang.org/t/how-to-use-toml-edit-to-insert-keys-in-table/128503)
- [DocumentMut struct documentation](https://docs.rs/toml_edit/latest/toml_edit/struct.DocumentMut.html)
- [Array API documentation](https://docs.rs/toml_edit/latest/toml_edit/struct.Array.html)
- [Table API documentation](https://docs.rs/toml_edit/latest/toml_edit/struct.Table.html)

They don't modify content, just copy files. Compute hash from serde path (for filename), but write original bytes.\n\n3. **toml_edit**: For `ts set`, `ts unset`, and future array add/remove.      1→//! `tspec ts set` - Set a scalar value in a tspec
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     7→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     8→use crate::tspec::{load_spec, save_spec};
     9→use crate::types::{OptLevel, PanicStrategy, Profile, Spec};
    10→
    11→/// Set a value in a tspec and save in place
    12→pub fn set_value(
    13→    project_root: &Path,
    14→    package: Option<&str>,
    15→    key: &str,
    16→    value: &str,
    17→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    18→) -> Result<()> {
    19→    let workspace = project_root;
    20→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    21→
    22→    // Load existing spec and its path, or use default with constructed path
    23→    let (mut spec, output_path) = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    24→        Some(path) => (load_spec(&path)?, path),
    25→        None => {
    26→            let base_name = match tspec {
    27→                Some(t) => t
    28→                    .strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    29→                    .or_else(|| t.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    30→                    .unwrap_or(t),
    31→                None => "tspec",
    32→            };
    33→            let path = package_dir.join(format!("{}{}", base_name, crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX));
    34→            (Spec::default(), path)
    35→        }
    36→    };
    37→
    38→    // Apply the key=value change
    39→    apply_value(&mut spec, key, value)?;
    40→
    41→    // Save in place
    42→    save_spec(&spec, &output_path)?;
    43→
    44→    println!(
    45→        "Saved {}",
    46→        output_path
    47→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    48→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    49→            .display()
    50→    );
    51→
    52→    Ok(())
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Apply key=value to a spec
    56→fn apply_value(spec: &mut Spec, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    57→    match key {
    58→        // Top-level high-level options
    59→        "panic" => {
    60→            spec.panic = Some(parse_panic_mode(value)?);
    61→        }
    62→        "strip" => {
    63→            spec.strip = Some(parse_strip_mode(value)?);
    64→        }
    65→
    66→        // Cargo config
    67→        "cargo.profile" => {
    68→            spec.cargo.profile = Some(parse_profile(value)?);
    69→        }
    70→        "cargo.target_triple" => {
    71→            spec.cargo.target_triple = Some(value.to_string());
    72→        }
    73→        "cargo.target_dir" => {
    74→            spec.cargo.target_dir = Some(value.to_string());
    75→        }
    76→
    77→        // Rustc config
    78→        "rustc.opt_level" => {
    79→            spec.rustc.opt_level = Some(parse_opt_level(value)?);
    80→        }
    81→        "rustc.panic" => {
    82→            spec.rustc.panic = Some(parse_panic_strategy(value)?);
    83→        }
    84→        "rustc.lto" => {
    85→            spec.rustc.lto = Some(parse_bool(value)?);
    86→        }
    87→        "rustc.codegen_units" => {
    88→            spec.rustc.codegen_units = Some(
    89→                value
    90→                    .parse()
    91→                    .with_context(|| format!("invalid codegen_units: {}", value))?,
    92→            );
    93→        }
    94→
    95→        _ => bail!("unknown key: {}", key),
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    Ok(())
    99→}
   100→
   101→fn parse_panic_mode(s: &str) -> Result<PanicMode> {
   102→    match s {
   103→        "unwind" => Ok(PanicMode::Unwind),
   104→        "abort" => Ok(PanicMode::Abort),
   105→        "immediate-abort" => Ok(PanicMode::ImmediateAbort),
   106→        _ => bail!(
   107→            "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
   108→            s
   109→        ),
   110→    }
   111→}
   112→
   113→fn parse_strip_mode(s: &str) -> Result<StripMode> {
   114→    match s {
   115→        "none" => Ok(StripMode::None),
   116→        "debuginfo" => Ok(StripMode::Debuginfo),
   117→        "symbols" => Ok(StripMode::Symbols),
   118→        _ => bail!(
   119→            "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
   120→            s
   121→        ),
   122→    }
   123→}
   124→
   125→fn parse_profile(s: &str) -> Result<Profile> {
   126→    match s {
   127→        "debug" => Ok(Profile::Debug),
   128→        "release" => Ok(Profile::Release),
   129→        _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", s),
   130→    }
   131→}
   132→
   133→fn parse_opt_level(s: &str) -> Result<OptLevel> {
   134→    match s {
   135→        "0" => Ok(OptLevel::O0),
   136→        "1" => Ok(OptLevel::O1),
   137→        "2" => Ok(OptLevel::O2),
   138→        "3" => Ok(OptLevel::O3),
   139→        "s" => Ok(OptLevel::Os),
   140→        "z" => Ok(OptLevel::Oz),
   141→        _ => bail!("invalid opt-level: {} (expected: 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z)", s),
   142→    }
   143→}
   144→
   145→fn parse_panic_strategy(s: &str) -> Result<PanicStrategy> {
   146→    match s {
   147→        "abort" => Ok(PanicStrategy::Abort),
   148→        "unwind" => Ok(PanicStrategy::Unwind),
   149→        "immediate-abort" => Ok(PanicStrategy::ImmediateAbort),
   150→        _ => bail!(
   151→            "invalid panic strategy: {} (expected: abort, unwind, immediate-abort)",
   152→            s
   153→        ),
   154→    }
   155→}
   156→
   157→fn parse_bool(s: &str) -> Result<bool> {
   158→    match s {
   159→        "true" | "yes" | "1" => Ok(true),
   160→        "false" | "no" | "0" => Ok(false),
   161→        _ => bail!("invalid boolean: {} (expected: true/false)", s),
   162→    }
   163→}
   164→

     1→//! `tspec ts backup` - Create a versioned backup of a tspec
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     7→use crate::tspec::{load_spec, save_spec_snapshot, spec_name_from_path};
     8→
     9→/// Create a versioned backup snapshot of a tspec
    10→pub fn backup_tspec(project_root: &Path, package: Option<&str>, tspec: Option<&str>) -> Result<()> {
    11→    let workspace = project_root;
    12→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    13→
    14→    let spec_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    15→        Some(path) => path,
    16→        None => bail!("no tspec found to backup"),
    17→    };
    18→
    19→    let spec = load_spec(&spec_path)?;
    20→    let base_name = spec_name_from_path(&spec_path);
    21→    let backup_path = save_spec_snapshot(&spec, &base_name, &package_dir)?;
    22→
    23→    println!(
    24→        "Backed up to {}",
    25→        backup_path
    26→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    27→            .unwrap_or(&backup_path)
    28→            .display()
    29→    );
    30→
    31→    Ok(())
    32→}
    33→

     1→//! `tspec ts restore` - Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     8→use crate::tspec::{load_spec, save_spec};
     9→
    10→/// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup to its base name
    11→pub fn restore_tspec(project_root: &Path, package: Option<&str>, tspec: &str) -> Result<()> {
    12→    let workspace = project_root;
    13→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    14→
    15→    let backup_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, Some(tspec))? {
    16→        Some(path) => path,
    17→        None => bail!("backup tspec not found: {}", tspec),
    18→    };
    19→
    20→    let base_name = parse_backup_base_name(&backup_path)?;
    21→    let target_path = package_dir.join(format!("{}{}", base_name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
    22→
    23→    let spec = load_spec(&backup_path)?;
    24→    save_spec(&spec, &target_path)?;
    25→
    26→    println!(
    27→        "Restored {} from {}",
    28→        target_path
    29→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    30→            .unwrap_or(&target_path)
    31→            .display(),
    32→        backup_path
    33→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    34→            .unwrap_or(&backup_path)
    35→            .display()
    36→    );
    37→
    38→    Ok(())
    39→}
    40→
    41→/// Parse a backup filename to extract the base name.
    42→/// Backup filenames have the pattern `{base}-{NNN}-{HHHHHHHH}.ts.toml`
    43→/// where NNN is a 3-digit sequence and HHHHHHHH is an 8-char hex hash.
    44→fn parse_backup_base_name(path: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    45→    let filename = path
    46→        .file_name()
    47→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    48→        .unwrap_or_default();
    49→
    50→    let stem = filename
    51→        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    52→        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    53→        .unwrap_or(&filename);
    54→
    55→    // Look for trailing -{NNN}-{HHHHHHHH} pattern
    56→    // Split from the right: last segment is hash (8 hex chars), second-to-last is seq (3 digits)
    57→    let parts: Vec<&str> = stem.rsplitn(3, '-').collect();
    58→    if parts.len() == 3 {
    59→        let hash_part = parts[0];
    60→        let seq_part = parts[1];
    61→        let base_part = parts[2];
    62→
    63→        if seq_part.len() == 3
    64→            && seq_part.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_digit())
    65→            && hash_part.len() == 8
    66→            && hash_part.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit())
    67→            && !base_part.is_empty()
    68→        {
    69→            return Ok(base_part.to_string());
    70→        }
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    bail!(
    74→        "not a backup filename (expected {{name}}-NNN-HHHHHHHH{}): {}",
    75→        TSPEC_SUFFIX,
    76→        filename
    77→    )
    78→}
    79→
    80→#[cfg(test)]
    81→mod tests {
    82→    use super::*;
    83→    use std::path::PathBuf;
    84→
    85→    #[test]
    86→    fn parse_simple_backup_name() {
    87→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml");
    88→        assert_eq!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).unwrap(), "t2");
    89→    }
    90→
    91→    #[test]
    92→    fn parse_backup_name_with_hyphens() {
    93→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/my-spec-name-003-deadbeef.ts.toml");
    94→        assert_eq!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).unwrap(), "my-spec-name");
    95→    }
    96→
    97→    #[test]
    98→    fn parse_backup_name_with_dots() {
    99→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/tspec.static-opt-001-12345678.ts.toml");
   100→        assert_eq!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).unwrap(), "tspec.static-opt");
   101→    }
   102→
   103→    #[test]
   104→    fn reject_non_backup_name() {
   105→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/t2.ts.toml");
   106→        assert!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).is_err());
   107→    }
   108→
   109→    #[test]
   110→    fn reject_wrong_seq_length() {
   111→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/t2-01-abcd1234.ts.toml");
   112→        assert!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).is_err());
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    #[test]
   116→    fn reject_wrong_hash_length() {
   117→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/t2-001-abcd.ts.toml");
   118→        assert!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).is_err());
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    #[test]
   122→    fn reject_non_hex_hash() {
   123→        let path = PathBuf::from("/dir/t2-001-ghijklmn.ts.toml");
   124→        assert!(parse_backup_base_name(&path).is_err());
   125→    }
   126→}

     1→//! `tspec ts new` - Create a new tspec file
     2→
     3→use anyhow::Result;
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     7→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     8→use crate::tspec::{load_spec, save_spec};
     9→use crate::types::Spec;
    10→
    11→/// Create a new tspec file (public entry point)
    12→pub fn new_tspec(
    13→    project_root: &Path,
    14→    package: Option<&str>,
    15→    name: &str,
    16→    from: Option<&str>,
    17→) -> Result<()> {
    18→    let workspace = project_root;
    19→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    20→
    21→    // Resolve source spec if --from provided
    22→    let source_spec = match from {
    23→        Some(source) => {
    24→            let source_path = resolve_source_spec(workspace, &package_dir, source)?;
    25→            Some(load_spec(&source_path)?)
    26→        }
    27→        None => None,
    28→    };
    29→
    30→    create_tspec_file(workspace, &package_dir, name, source_spec.as_ref())
    31→}
    32→
    33→/// Resolve the source spec path from a --from argument
    34→fn resolve_source_spec(
    35→    workspace: &Path,
    36→    current_package_dir: &Path,
    37→    source: &str,
    38→) -> Result<std::path::PathBuf> {
    39→    // Parse source: could be "package/spec" or just "spec"
    40→    let (source_package_dir, source_spec, source_name) = if source.contains('/') {
    41→        let parts: Vec<&str> = source.splitn(2, '/').collect();
    42→        let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(workspace, parts[0])?;
    43→        (pkg_dir, Some(parts[1]), parts[0])
    44→    } else {
    45→        // Same package, just spec name
    46→        (
    47→            current_package_dir.to_path_buf(),
    48→            Some(source),
    49→            "current package",
    50→        )
    51→    };
    52→
    53→    find_tspec(&source_package_dir, source_spec)?.ok_or_else(|| {
    54→        anyhow::anyhow!(
    55→            "source tspec '{}' not found in {}",
    56→            source_spec.unwrap_or("tspec"),
    57→            source_name
    58→        )
    59→    })
    60→}
    61→
    62→/// Create a tspec file - core logic, testable
    63→fn create_tspec_file(
    64→    workspace: &Path,
    65→    crate_dir: &Path,
    66→    name: &str,
    67→    source_spec: Option<&Spec>,
    68→) -> Result<()> {
    69→    let spec = source_spec.cloned().unwrap_or_default();
    70→    let output_path = crate_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
    71→
    72→    // Check if file already exists
    73→    if output_path.exists() {
    74→        anyhow::bail!(
    75→            "tspec '{}' already exists. Use a different name or delete the existing file.",
    76→            output_path.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy(),
    77→        );
    78→    }
    79→
    80→    // Save the spec
    81→    save_spec(&spec, &output_path)?;
    82→
    83→    println!(
    84→        "Created {}",
    85→        output_path
    86→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    87→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    88→            .display()
    89→    );
    90→
    91→    Ok(())
    92→}
    93→
    94→#[cfg(test)]
    95→mod tests {
    96→    use super::*;
    97→    use crate::test_constants::SUFFIX;
    98→    use crate::types::{CargoConfig, LinkerConfig, Profile};
    99→    use tempfile::TempDir;
   100→
   101→    #[test]
   102→    fn create_empty_tspec() {
   103→        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   104→        let crate_dir = dir.path();
   105→
   106→        create_tspec_file(dir.path(), crate_dir, "test", None).unwrap();
   107→
   108→        let created = crate_dir.join(format!("test{}", SUFFIX));
   109→        assert!(created.exists());
   110→
   111→        let spec = load_spec(&created).unwrap();
   112→        assert_eq!(spec, Spec::default());
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    #[test]
   116→    fn create_tspec_from_source() {
   117→        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
   118→        let crate_dir = dir.path();
   119→
   120→        let source = Spec {
   121→            cargo: CargoConfig {
   122→                profile: Some(Profile::Release),
   123→                ..Default::default()
   124→            },
   125→            linker: LinkerConfig {
   126→                args: vec!["-static".to_string()],
   127→                ..Default::default()
   128→            },
   129→            ..Default::default()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        create_tspec_file(dir.path(), crate_dir, "copy", Some(&source)).unwrap();
   133→
   134→        let created = crate_dir.join(format!("copy{}", SUFFIX));
   135→        assert!(created.exists());
   136→
   137→        let loaded = load_spec(&created).unwrap();
   138→        assert_eq!(loaded.cargo.profile, Some(Profile::Release));
   139→        assert_eq!(loaded.linker.args, vec!["-static".to_string()]);
   140→    }

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     2→use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::Spec;
     7→
     8→/// Load a spec from a TOML file
     9→pub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec> {
    10→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
    11→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
    12→    parse_spec(&content)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Parse a spec from TOML string
    16→pub fn parse_spec(toml_str: &str) -> Result<Spec> {
    17→    toml::from_str(toml_str).context("failed to parse spec TOML")
    18→}
    19→
    20→/// Serialize a spec to TOML string (canonical form for hashing)
    21→pub fn serialize_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    22→    toml::to_string(spec).context("failed to serialize spec")
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Compute hash of a resolved spec (first 8 hex chars)
    26→pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    27→    let toml_str = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    28→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
    29→    hasher.update(toml_str.as_bytes());
    30→    let result = hasher.finalize();
    31→    Ok(hex::encode(&result[..4]))
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Extract spec name from path by stripping the .ts.toml (or .toml) suffix
    35→pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {
    36→    let filename = path
    37→        .file_name()
    38→        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
    39→        .unwrap_or_default();
    40→
    41→    filename
    42→        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    43→        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    44→        .unwrap_or(&filename)
    45→        .to_string()
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Expand template placeholders in a spec's target_dir field.
    49→/// Returns None if target_dir is absent or empty.
    50→pub fn expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    51→    let raw = match &spec.cargo.target_dir {
    52→        Some(td) if !td.is_empty() => td,
    53→        _ => return Ok(None),
    54→    };
    55→
    56→    let mut expanded = raw.clone();
    57→
    58→    if expanded.contains("<name>") {
    59→        expanded = expanded.replace("<name>", spec_name);
    60→    }
    61→
    62→    if expanded.contains("<hash>") {
    63→        let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
    64→        expanded = expanded.replace("<hash>", &hash);
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    if expanded.is_empty() {
    68→        Ok(None)
    69→    } else {
    70→        Ok(Some(expanded))
    71→    }
    72→}
    73→
    74→/// Save a spec to a TOML file, creating parent directories if needed
    75→pub fn save_spec(spec: &Spec, path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    76→    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
    77→        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
    78→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", parent.display()))?;
    79→    }
    80→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    81→    std::fs::write(path, &content)
    82→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Save a spec snapshot with sequence number and hash: `{name}-{seq:03}-{hash}.toml`
    86→/// Returns the path of the created file.
    87→pub fn save_spec_snapshot(spec: &Spec, name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {
    88→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)
    89→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", dir.display()))?;
    90→
    91→    // Find highest existing sequence number for this name
    92→    let prefix = format!("{}-", name);
    93→    let next_seq = std::fs::read_dir(dir)
    94→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read directory: {}", dir.display()))?
    95→        .filter_map(|entry| entry.ok())
    96→        .filter_map(|entry| {
    97→            let filename = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    98→            if filename.starts_with(&prefix) && filename.ends_with(TSPEC_SUFFIX) {
    99→                // Extract sequence number: {name}-{seq:03}-{hash}{TSPEC_SUFFIX}
   100→                let rest = filename.strip_prefix(&prefix)?;
   101→                let seq_str = rest.split('-').next()?;
   102→                seq_str.parse::<u32>().ok()
   103→            } else {
   104→                None
   105→            }
   106→        })
   107→        .max()
   108→        .map(|n| n + 1)
   109→        .unwrap_or(1);
   110→
   111→    let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
   112→    let filename = format!("{}-{:03}-{}{}", name, next_seq, hash, TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   113→    let path = dir.join(&filename);
   114→
   115→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
   116→    std::fs::write(&path, &content)
   117→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
   118→
   119→    Ok(path)
   120→}
   121→

   200→    #[test]

   211→
   212→        let path1 = save_spec_snapshot(&spec1, "test", dir.path()).unwrap();
   213→        let path2 = save_spec_snapshot(&spec2, "test", dir.path()).unwrap();
   214→        let path3 = save_spec_snapshot(&spec1, "test", dir.path()).unwrap();
   215→
   216→        let name1 = path1.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
   217→        let name2 = path2.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy();

   223→        assert!(name1.ends_with(SUFFIX), "got: {}", name1);
   224→
   225→        // Same content = same hash suffix (strip prefix and SUFFIX)

     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::{Args, Subcommand};
     3→use std::path::Path;

     6→use super::Execute;

     8→
     9→/// Manage translation specs
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct TsCmd {
    12→    #[command(subcommand)]
    13→    command: TsCommands,
    14→}
    15→
    16→#[derive(Subcommand)]
    17→pub enum TsCommands {
    18→    /// List tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    19→    List {
    20→        /// Package to list specs for (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→        package: Option<String>,
    23→        /// List all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→        all: bool,
    26→    },
    27→    /// Show a tspec's contents
    28→    Show {
    29→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    30→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    31→        package: Option<String>,
    32→        /// Show all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    33→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    34→        all: bool,
    35→        /// Tspec name (defaults to all tspec files)
    36→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    37→        tspec: Option<String>,
    38→    },
    39→    /// Show the content hash of a tspec
    40→    Hash {
    41→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    42→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    43→        package: Option<String>,
    44→        /// Hash all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    45→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    46→        all: bool,
    47→        /// Tspec name (defaults to package's tspec file)
    48→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    49→        tspec: Option<String>,
    50→    },
    51→    /// Create a new tspec file
    52→    New {
    53→        /// Name for the new tspec (default: "tspec")
    54→        #[arg(default_value = "tspec")]
    55→        name: String,
    56→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    57→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    58→        package: Option<String>,
    59→        /// Copy from existing tspec (package/spec or just spec name in same package)
    60→        #[arg(short = 'f', long = "from")]
    61→        from: Option<String>,
    62→    },
    63→    /// Set a scalar value in a tspec (modifies in place)
    64→    Set {
    65→        /// Key=value pair (e.g., "strip=symbols", "panic=abort", "rustc.lto=true")
    66→        assignment: String,
    67→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    68→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    69→        package: Option<String>,
    70→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    71→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    72→        tspec: Option<String>,
    73→    },
    74→    /// Create a versioned backup of a tspec
    75→    Backup {
    76→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    77→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    78→        package: Option<String>,
    79→        /// Tspec to backup (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    80→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    81→        tspec: Option<String>,
    82→    },
    83→    /// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
    84→    Restore {
    85→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    86→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    87→        package: Option<String>,
    88→        /// Backup tspec to restore from (required, e.g., "t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml")
    89→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    90→        tspec: String,
    91→    },
    92→}
    93→
    94→impl Execute for TsCmd {
    95→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    96→        match &self.command {
    97→            TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
    98→                ts_cmd::list_tspecs(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all)?;
    99→            }
   100→            TsCommands::Show {
   101→                package,
   102→                all,
   103→                tspec,
   104→            } => {
   105→                ts_cmd::show_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   106→            }
   107→            TsCommands::Hash {
   108→                package,
   109→                all,
   110→                tspec,
   111→            } => {
   112→                ts_cmd::hash_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   113→            }
   114→            TsCommands::New {
   115→                name,
   116→                package,
   117→                from,
   118→            } => {
   119→                ts_cmd::new_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), name, from.as_deref())?;
   120→            }
   121→            TsCommands::Set {
   122→                assignment,
   123→                package,
   124→                tspec,
   125→            } => {
   126→                let (key, value) = assignment.split_once('=').ok_or_else(|| {
   127→                    anyhow::anyhow!("invalid assignment '{}': expected key=value", assignment)
   128→                })?;
   129→                ts_cmd::set_value(
   130→                    project_root,
   131→                    package.as_deref(),
   132→                    key,
   133→                    value,
   134→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   135→                )?;
   136→            }
   137→            TsCommands::Backup { package, tspec } => {
   138→                ts_cmd::backup_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec.as_deref())?;
   139→            }
   140→            TsCommands::Restore { package, tspec } => {
   141→                ts_cmd::restore_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec)?;
   142→            }
   143→        }
   144→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   145→    }
   146→}

     1→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
     2→use std::path::PathBuf;

     4→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     5→
     6→/// Build profile - mutually exclusive
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8→#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
     9→pub enum Profile {
    10→    Debug,
    11→    Release,
    12→}
    13→
    14→/// Optimization level - mutually exclusive
    15→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    16→pub enum OptLevel {
    17→    #[serde(rename = "0")]
    18→    O0,
    19→    #[serde(rename = "1")]
    20→    O1,
    21→    #[serde(rename = "2")]
    22→    O2,
    23→    #[serde(rename = "3")]
    24→    O3,
    25→    #[serde(rename = "s")]
    26→    Os,
    27→    #[serde(rename = "z")]
    28→    Oz,
    29→}
    30→
    31→/// Panic strategy - mutually exclusive
    32→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    33→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    34→pub enum PanicStrategy {
    35→    Abort,
    36→    Unwind,
    37→    /// Nightly only: -C panic=immediate-abort (eliminates panic formatting)
    38→    ImmediateAbort,
    39→}
    40→
    41→/// Cargo-specific configuration (flat struct)
    42→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    43→pub struct CargoConfig {
    44→    /// Build profile (debug or release)
    45→    pub profile: Option<Profile>,
    46→    /// Target triple (e.g., "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl")
    47→    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    48→    /// Custom target JSON file path
    49→    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    50→    /// Nightly-only -Z flags (e.g., ["panic-immediate-abort"])
    51→    #[serde(default)]
    52→    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    53→    /// Custom target directory subdirectory for per-spec isolation.
    54→    /// Supports `<name>` (spec filename sans .ts.toml) and `<hash>` (8-char content hash).
    55→    pub target_dir: Option<String>,
    56→}
    57→
    58→/// Rustc codegen and compilation configuration (flat struct)
    59→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    60→pub struct RustcConfig {
    61→    /// Optimization level (-C opt-level=)
    62→    pub opt_level: Option<OptLevel>,
    63→    /// Panic strategy (-C panic=)
    64→    pub panic: Option<PanicStrategy>,
    65→    /// Enable LTO (-C lto=true)
    66→    pub lto: Option<bool>,
    67→    /// Codegen units (-C codegen-units=)
    68→    pub codegen_units: Option<u32>,
    69→    /// Crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly only)
    70→    #[serde(default)]
    71→    pub build_std: Vec<String>,
    72→    /// Raw flags passed through
    73→    #[serde(default)]
    74→    pub flags: Vec<String>,
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Version script configuration for symbol visibility
    78→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    79→pub struct VersionScript {
    80→    /// Symbols to keep global (e.g., ["_start"])
    81→    pub global: Vec<String>,
    82→    /// Pattern for local symbols (typically "*")
    83→    #[serde(default = "default_local")]
    84→    pub local: String,
    85→}

   100→
   101→/// A translation spec
   102→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   103→pub struct Spec {
   104→    /// High-level panic mode (sets both cargo -Z and rustc -C flags)
   105→    pub panic: Option<PanicMode>,

     1→//! High-level build options that expand to lower-level flags.
     2→//!
     3→//! These provide "easy generality" - user picks intent, implementation
     4→//! details (which cargo/rustc flags to set) are handled automatically.
     5→
     6→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
     7→
     8→/// Panic handling strategy.
     9→///
    10→/// This is a high-level option that automatically sets the appropriate
    11→/// cargo and rustc flags.
    12→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    13→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    14→pub enum PanicMode {
    15→    /// Default Rust behavior - panic unwinds the stack.
    16→    #[default]
    17→    Unwind,
    18→
    19→    /// Panic aborts immediately (no unwinding).
    20→    /// Sets: rustc -C panic=abort
    21→    Abort,
    22→
    23→    /// Panic aborts with no formatting overhead (nightly only).
    24→    /// Sets: cargo -Z panic-immediate-abort AND rustc -C panic=immediate-abort
    25→    /// This eliminates all panic formatting machinery for smallest binaries.
    26→    ImmediateAbort,
    27→}
    28→
    29→impl PanicMode {
    30→    /// Returns true if this mode requires nightly toolchain.
    31→    pub fn requires_nightly(&self) -> bool {
    32→        matches!(self, PanicMode::ImmediateAbort)
    33→    }
    34→
    35→    /// Returns the cargo -Z flag if needed.
    36→    pub fn cargo_z_flag(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
    37→        match self {
    38→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort => Some("panic-immediate-abort"),
    39→            _ => None,
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→
    43→    /// Returns the rustc -C panic= value.
    44→    pub fn rustc_panic_value(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
    45→        match self {
    46→            PanicMode::Unwind => None, // default, no flag needed
    47→            PanicMode::Abort => Some("abort"),
    48→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort => Some("immediate-abort"),
    49→        }
    50→    }
    51→}
    52→
    53→/// Symbol stripping mode.
    54→///
    55→/// This is a high-level option that sets the rustc strip flag.
    56→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    57→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    58→pub enum StripMode {
    59→    /// No stripping (default).
    60→    #[default]
    61→    None,
    62→
    63→    /// Strip debug info only.
    64→    /// Sets: rustc -C strip=debuginfo
    65→    Debuginfo,
    66→
    67→    /// Strip all symbols.
    68→    /// Sets: rustc -C strip=symbols
    69→    Symbols,
    70→}
    71→
    72→impl StripMode {
    73→    /// Returns the rustc -C strip= value, if any.
    74→    pub fn rustc_strip_value(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
    75→        match self {
    76→            StripMode::None => None,
    77→            StripMode::Debuginfo => Some("debuginfo"),
    78→            StripMode::Symbols => Some("symbols"),
    79→        }
    80→    }
    81→}
    82→
    83→#[cfg(test)]
    84→mod tests {
    85→    use super::*;
    86→
    87→    #[test]
    88→    fn unwind_is_default() {
    89→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::default(), PanicMode::Unwind);
    90→    }
    91→
    92→    #[test]
    93→    fn only_immediate_abort_requires_nightly() {
    94→        assert!(!PanicMode::Unwind.requires_nightly());
    95→        assert!(!PanicMode::Abort.requires_nightly());
    96→        assert!(PanicMode::ImmediateAbort.requires_nightly());
    97→    }
    98→
    99→    #[test]
   100→    fn cargo_z_flag_only_for_immediate_abort() {
   101→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Unwind.cargo_z_flag(), None);
   102→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Abort.cargo_z_flag(), None);
   103→        assert_eq!(
   104→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort.cargo_z_flag(),
   105→            Some("panic-immediate-abort")
   106→        );
   107→    }
   108→
   109→    #[test]
   110→    fn rustc_panic_values() {
   111→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Unwind.rustc_panic_value(), None);
   112→        assert_eq!(PanicMode::Abort.rustc_panic_value(), Some("abort"));
   113→        assert_eq!(
   114→            PanicMode::ImmediateAbort.rustc_panic_value(),
   115→            Some("immediate-abort")
   116→        );
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    #[test]
   120→    fn strip_none_is_default() {
   121→        assert_eq!(StripMode::default(), StripMode::None);
   122→    }
   123→
   124→    #[test]
   125→    fn rustc_strip_values() {
   126→        assert_eq!(StripMode::None.rustc_strip_value(), None);
   127→        assert_eq!(StripMode::Debuginfo.rustc_strip_value(), Some("debuginfo"));
   128→        assert_eq!(StripMode::Symbols.rustc_strip_value(), Some("symbols"));
   129→    }
   130→}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/.claude/plans/imperative-skipping-sky.md",
  "content": "# Plan: ts set/unset/backup/restore improvements using toml_edit\n\n## Context\n\n`ts set` currently only supports ~10 hardcoded scalar keys and silently ignores all array/nested fields (`rustc.build_std`, `rustc.flags`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable`, `cargo.target_json`, `linker.version_script`). Users get `Error: unknown key` for documented fields. Additionally, `ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` round-trip through serde serialization, which destroys user comments and formatting.\n\n### Three write strategies\n\n| Operation | Strategy | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |\n| set, unset | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |\n| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |\n\n## Steps\n\n### Step 1: Add `toml_edit` dependency\n\n**File:** `Cargo.toml`\n\nAdd `toml_edit = \"0.22\"` to `[dependencies]`. Already a transitive dep via `toml 0.8`, so no new download.\n\n### Step 2: Create `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs` - toml_edit helper module\n\nNew module with:\n\n- **`FIELD_REGISTRY`** - static list of all valid field paths with their types:\n  ```\n  (\"panic\", Scalar)           (\"strip\", Scalar)\n  (\"cargo.profile\", Scalar)   (\"cargo.target_triple\", Scalar)\n  (\"cargo.target_json\", Scalar) (\"cargo.target_dir\", Scalar)\n  (\"cargo.unstable\", Array)   (\"rustc.opt_level\", Scalar)\n  (\"rustc.panic\", Scalar)     (\"rustc.lto\", Scalar)\n  (\"rustc.codegen_units\", Scalar) (\"rustc.build_std\", Array)\n  (\"rustc.flags\", Array)      (\"linker.args\", Array)\n  ```\n  (Skip `linker.version_script` for now - it's a nested table with `global` array + `local` string, more complex.)\n\n- **`validate_key(key) -> Result<FieldKind>`** - checks key against registry, returns `Scalar` or `Array`\n\n- **`set_field(doc, key, value) -> Result<()>`** - for scalars: parse key path → ensure table exists → set `doc[\"section\"][\"field\"] = value`. Value parsing: booleans as `toml_edit::value(true/false)`, integers as `toml_edit::value(i64)`, everything else as string. For arrays: parse `[a,b,c]` or `\"a\",\"b\"` syntax → replace the array.\n\n- **`unset_field(doc, key) -> Result<()>`** - parse key path → remove from table. If table becomes empty, remove the table too.\n\n- **`validate_value(key, value) -> Result<()>`** - reuse existing parse functions (`parse_panic_mode`, `parse_strip_mode`, etc.) for enum keys. Accept anything for string/array keys.\n\n### Step 3: Rewrite `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` to use toml_edit\n\nReplace serde round-trip with:\n1. Read file as raw string (or empty string if file doesn't exist)\n2. Parse into `DocumentMut`\n3. Validate key via `edit::validate_key()`\n4. Validate value via `edit::validate_value()` (for enum fields)\n5. Call `edit::set_field(doc, key, value)`\n6. Write `doc.to_string()` back to file\n\nRemove `apply_value()` and all parse helper functions from set.rs (move validation to edit.rs).\n\nUpdate tests to test the new toml_edit-based flow. Add tests for previously-missing keys (`rustc.build_std`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable`, etc.).\n\n### Step 4: Create `src/ts_cmd/unset.rs` - new command\n\nNew file:\n1. Resolve package dir and tspec path (same pattern as set.rs)\n2. Read file as raw string\n3. Parse into `DocumentMut`\n4. Validate key via `edit::validate_key()`\n5. Call `edit::unset_field(doc, key)`\n6. Write back\n\n### Step 5: Wire up `ts unset` in CLI\n\n**Files:** `src/cmd/ts.rs`, `src/ts_cmd/mod.rs`\n\n- Add `Unset` variant to `TsCommands` enum (same args as `Set` minus the value):\n  ```\n  Unset { key, package, tspec }\n  ```\n- Add `unset_value` to `ts_cmd/mod.rs` re-exports\n- Add match arm in `TsCmd::execute()`\n\n### Step 6: Convert `ts backup` to raw file copy\n\n**Files:** `src/ts_cmd/backup.rs`, `src/tspec.rs`\n\nCurrent flow: `load_spec` → `save_spec_snapshot` (serde serialize) - destroys comments.\n\nNew flow:\n1. Find the spec path (same as now)\n2. `load_spec` **only for hashing** (to compute the hash for the backup filename)\n3. Compute next sequence number (same logic as `save_spec_snapshot`)\n4. `fs::copy(source_path, backup_path)` - exact byte-for-byte copy\n\nExtract the sequence-number logic from `save_spec_snapshot` into a helper `next_snapshot_seq(name, dir)` in `tspec.rs` so both old code and new backup can use it.\n\n### Step 7: Convert `ts restore` to raw file copy\n\n**File:** `src/ts_cmd/restore.rs`\n\nCurrent flow: `load_spec(backup)` → `save_spec(target)` - destroys comments.\n\nNew flow:\n1. Find backup path (same as now)\n2. Parse base name (same as now)\n3. `fs::copy(backup_path, target_path)` - exact copy\n\n### Step 8: Convert `ts new -f` to raw file copy\n\n**File:** `src/ts_cmd/new.rs`\n\nCurrent flow: `load_spec(source)` → `save_spec(target)` - destroys comments.\n\nNew flow when `--from` is provided:\n1. Resolve source spec path (same as now)\n2. `fs::copy(source_path, output_path)` - exact copy\n\nWhen `--from` is NOT provided, keep using serde `save_spec(&Spec::default(), ...)` since there's no source to copy.\n\n### Step 9 (nice-to-have): Array append/remove syntax in `ts set`\n\nExtend `ts set` to support:\n- `tspec ts set linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections` (append to array)\n- `tspec ts set linker.args-=-Wl,--gc-sections` (remove from array)\n- `tspec ts set linker.args=[\"-static\",\"-nostdlib\"]` (replace entire array, from step 3)\n\nThis builds on the `edit::set_field` infrastructure. Can be deferred if the basic set/unset is solid.\n\n### Step 10: Update todo.md\n\nMark completed items, remove resolved entries.\n\n## Files to modify\n\n| File | Change |\n|---|---|\n| `Cargo.toml` | Add `toml_edit` dep |\n| `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs` | **NEW** - toml_edit helpers |\n| `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` | Rewrite to use toml_edit |\n| `src/ts_cmd/unset.rs` | **NEW** - unset command |\n| `src/ts_cmd/mod.rs` | Add unset + edit modules |\n| `src/cmd/ts.rs` | Add Unset variant |\n| `src/ts_cmd/backup.rs` | Use fs::copy |\n| `src/ts_cmd/restore.rs` | Use fs::copy |\n| `src/ts_cmd/new.rs` | Use fs::copy for --from |\n| `src/tspec.rs` | Extract `next_snapshot_seq()` helper |\n| `notes/todo.md` | Update status |\n\n## Verification\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec          # All existing + new tests pass\ntspec clippy                 # No warnings\ntspec fmt --check            # Formatted\n\n# Manual smoke tests:\ntspec ts set rustc.build_std='[\"core\",\"alloc\"]'    # Previously: \"unknown key\"\ntspec ts set linker.args='[\"-static\"]'             # Previously: \"unknown key\"\ntspec ts unset rustc.lto                           # New command\ntspec ts backup && diff original backup            # Byte-identical\ntspec ts restore -t name-001-hash                  # Preserves comments\n```\n"
}
```

     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→`ts set` currently only supports ~10 hardcoded scalar keys and silently ignores all array/nested fields (`rustc.build_std`, `rustc.flags`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable`, `cargo.target_json`, `linker.version_script`).
